# M4 execution: task 8 (owner-approved, 2026-07-31)

Task 8 of the M4 wave A plan: `skeptic/mutation.py`, the pure module that
enumerates mutants over the changed functions (and, in `patch_plus_callers`
scope, their approximate callers) and samples a budgeted subset of them
deterministically. Nothing here executes a mutant; Task 9 runs what this
task produces.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 109 | Three narrow readings, one module. (a) **The operator set has six entries; the engineering plan's own list names seven.** `off_by_one` folds the engineering plan's separate "constant tweak" entry into itself, since both name "integer constant n -> n + 1"; `off_by_one` also reaches through a leading unary minus with no special case, because Python's own parser never folds a sign into a `Constant` node (`-1` parses as `UnaryOp(USub(), Constant(1))`), so the operator increments the constant's own stored value regardless of a `USub` wrapping it (`-1` becomes `-2`). `arithmetic_swap` does not touch `**`: the operator names two swapped pairs (`+ <-> -`, `* <-> //`), `Pow` has no partner in either, and inventing one would be a seventh transformation nobody asked for. (b) **The caller scan is name-based**, matching a `Call` whose callee is `ast.Name` or `ast.Attribute` against a changed function's own name, with no import resolution: a same-name method on an unrelated class over-includes, an aliased import under-includes, and both are bounded by the caller row's 0.25 weight (decision 4's sibling); `test_dirs`/`conftest.py` are excluded from the caller scan for the same reason they are excluded from changed spans, even though the brief only restates the cut for the latter. (c) **Sampling's stratum key substitutes `line` for "enclosing function".** `Mutant`'s shape, fixed by the brief, carries no function-identity field, and `sample_mutants` receives a bare `Sequence[Mutant]` with no spans and no tree, so it cannot look up which function a `line` sits inside after generation. The stratum key is `(0 if changed else 1, path, line, operator)`: finer-grained than true function-level grouping when one function is mutated at several lines, but every pinned sampling property (seed-determinism, seed-sensitivity, the budget cap, changed-before-caller ordering) holds under it exactly. | (a) An `off_by_one -> off_by_one` plus `constant_tweak` split would double-count one mutation kind as two rule ids under the frozen weight table, and Python's own parsing of a signed literal makes the "reach through the minus" behavior free rather than a deliberate widening. `arithmetic_swap` reaching `Pow` has no textual basis in the brief and no natural partner to swap it with. (b) Import-graph precision for the caller scan was already cut at the plan's v2 gate (decision 4's sibling); re-opening it here would be scope creep into Task 9's execution budget. (c) Threading true function identity through would need either a new `Mutant` field (out of scope: the brief's shape is the contract Task 9 codes against next) or a second return channel from `generate_mutants` that `sample_mutants`'s fixed two-argument signature has no room for. | Giving `off_by_one` a signed-literal special case that decrements a negative constant's magnitude toward zero instead of incrementing its stored value: the brief states no direction for the negative case, and the chosen reading needs no special-casing at all, which is the narrower of the two. Adding a `Pow` entry to `arithmetic_swap`'s flip table paired with `*` or `Mult`: no basis in the operator's own two-pairs name. Widening `Mutant` with a function-name field to make sampling's true intent literal: rejected pending Task 9's own review gate, since the shape here is what that task's brief already cites verbatim. |

**Ripple:** fast suite (`-m "not docker"`) goes from 359 to 378 passed (19
new `test_mutation.py` cases: the six-operator parametrization, the two
span-scoping tests, the three caller-scan tests, the four sampling tests,
the invalid-mutant test, the late-line test, and two extra narrow-reading
pins for the off-by-one/unary-minus and arithmetic-swap/power calls above).
No docker-marked test added (Task 8 needs no daemon), so the docker-deselected
count is unchanged from Task 7 at 45. `ruff check .` is clean.

**Review round 1 fix (2026-08-01).** Four findings from the task 8 review,
all ruled by the controller.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 110 | `Mutant` gains `function: str` (the enclosing function's qualified dotted name, `""` at module level, built by a dedicated recursive walk since `ast.walk`'s breadth-first order carries no parent information), and `sample_mutants`'s stratum key reverts to `(0 if changed else 1, path, function, operator)`. This corrects row 109(c), which had substituted the mutant's own `line` for "enclosing function" on the reasoning that `Mutant`'s brief-fixed shape carried no function-identity field. | The review measured 109(c)'s substitution directly rather than reasoning about it abstractly: a compact two-line function sitting beside a many-line one received zero mutants through budget 8 under the `line` key, because the sprawling function's many single-mutant `line` strata dominate every round-robin pass, while the function key gives the compact function both of its own two mutants by budget 2. 109(c)'s own worry, that a real function-identity field would somehow forfeit a pinned sampling property, never materialized: every sampling test (determinism, seed-sensitivity, the budget cap, changed-before-caller) holds unchanged once its fixtures vary `function` instead of `line` to form distinct strata. | Keeping the `line` substitution and treating the starvation as an acceptable tradeoff: the review's own probe, a short function beside a long one, is exactly the corpus shape mutation testing exists to cover evenly, and starving the short function of coverage is the more damaging failure mode of the two 109(c) weighed. A hybrid key such as `(path, line // N, operator)` for some bucket size N, approximating function grouping without a new field: rejected as an invented heuristic with no textual basis, when the real span-to-name machinery (`_changed_function_names`) already existed in this module, making a proper enclosing-function map a direct fix rather than an invented one. |

**Second fix in the same round: mutant_id collisions across populations.**
`caller_function_spans`'s own exclusion (a caller candidate whose span
exactly equals one of `changed`'s entries is dropped) only catches a
function reported as a caller in its own right. It misses a caller function
nested inside an already-changed enclosing function, and misses a changed
function nested inside an already-caller-eligible enclosing function, since
neither nested function's own span is the one excluded. The review's probe:
an `outer` function with a nested `inner` that calls an unrelated changed
function `helper`, with only one line of `outer` (not `inner`) diffed, so
`outer`'s whole span reads as "changed" while `inner` independently
qualifies as a caller. Before the fix this produced 12 mutants with only 9
unique ids: `inner`'s body was mutated once under "changed" (via `outer`'s
span) and once under "caller" (via `inner`'s own span), and since
`_mutant_id` hashes `(path, line, operator, occurrence)` with a per-call
occurrence counter, the two collided. Fixed in `generate_mutants`, not
`caller_function_spans`: when building caller-population mutants for a
path, any node whose line falls inside that same path's changed spans is
now skipped, regardless of which caller function span nominally contains
it. Fixing it at this node level rather than the span level is what makes
it symmetric across both nesting directions (inner-changed/outer-caller and
outer-changed/inner-caller), the second of which is the review's own probe
shape. `_mutant_id`'s definition is unchanged. Pinned by
`test_caller_pass_excludes_nodes_already_covered_by_a_changed_span`, which
asserts every id in the pair's full `generate_mutants` output is unique and
that the overlapping line is mutated only under "changed".

**Third fix in the same round: unguarded `ast.parse`.** All four call
sites (changed-span computation, caller-name recovery, the caller scan,
and mutant generation) called `ast.parse` directly, so an unparseable file
anywhere in the tree raised a bare `SyntaxError` out of a pure module
instead of the harness's own infra-failure shape, and looked, from the
caller's side, indistinguishable from an ordinary Python exception bubbling
out of a bug. All four now go through `_parse_file`, which raises
`SkepticInfraError` in the what/why/next form the rest of the codebase
uses (`skeptic/checks/observations.py`'s pattern), naming the offending
file. Pinned by `test_an_unparseable_changed_file_raises_skepticinfraerror`
and `test_an_unparseable_src_dirs_file_in_the_caller_scan_raises_skepticinfraerror`.

**Fourth fix in the same round: the per-pass round-robin reading, pinned.**
No code changed here: the existing round-robin already visits every
stratum once per pass in sorted order before revisiting any of them, which
is what makes a tight budget spend across every changed stratum once
before returning to one a second time. The review asked for a test that
would fail under a plausible alternative reading (exhaust one population's
strata completely before touching the other's) so the semantics cannot
drift silently later. `test_sampling_round_robins_per_pass_rather_than_exhausting_one_population_first`
gives two changed strata and two caller strata, three mutants each, at
budget 4, and pins the exact population sequence a per-pass round-robin
produces (`changed, changed, caller, caller`) against the sequence an
exhaust-first reading would have produced instead (`changed, changed,
changed, changed`).

**Ripple:** fast suite (`-m "not docker"`) goes from 378 to 382 passed (four
new `test_mutation.py` cases: the nested caller/changed collision pin, the
two parse-guard pins, and the per-pass-versus-exhaust-first pin), plus three
existing sampling tests updated in place to vary `function` instead of
`line` so they still form the distinct strata their assertions depend on.
No docker-marked test added. `ruff check .` is clean.

# M4 execution: task 9 (owner-approved, 2026-08-01)

Task 9 of the M4 wave A plan: mutation execution and `t2_mutation`, the
largest task. `skeptic/checks/observations.py` gains `MutantRecord`/
`MutationReport` and `VariantObservations.mutation`; `skeptic/mutation.py`
gains the context-to-nodeid bridge (`select_tests`) and the `FULL_SUITE`
sentinel; `skeptic/collector.py` gains `observe_mutation`, the batch runner;
`skeptic/checks/t2_mutation.py` is new, the pure fold from a `MutationReport`
to a `CheckResult`; `T2_REGISTRY` and `MANDATORY_CHECKS` both gain
`"t2_mutation"`; `skeptic/cli.py`'s `do_verify` enriches the pair between
`collect_pair` and `run_verify_layer`.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 111 | The bridge (`mutation.select_tests`) splits a coverage context's dotted string on `.`, treats the first segment as the module and the rest as the qualname, and matches the module against the nodeid file's stem (its final path component, without directory or extension) and the qualname against the nodeid's `::` parts with any `[param]` suffix stripped from the last one. A parametrized family collapses to every nodeid sharing that stripped qualname (superset selection, the brief's own spelling), and a non-empty context matching zero nodeids raises `SkepticInfraError` rather than falling back to anything. Verified against the corpus's own measured context shapes (`tests/fixtures/coverage/minirepo-gold/coverage.json`, and `collector.py`'s own docstring): coverage.py's `dynamic_context = test_function` writes `{module}.{qualname}` where `module` is the flat top-level name pytest imports a test file under, carrying no `tests.`-style package prefix, since no test dir in the corpus ships an `__init__.py`. Five named tests pin the module/function split, the three-part class-based split, the parametrized-family superset, both `None` shapes (the line absent from `coverage.contexts[path]` entirely, and present with every context empty), and the INFRA raise. | The corpus's own measured context strings settle where "module" ends and "qualname" begins: they never carry a directory-derived prefix, so the first dotted segment is the only value independently anchored (the nodeid file's own stem), and no ambiguity is left for a smarter split to resolve. Superset selection is the brief's explicit ruling: a covering test's context names only the unparametrized function, so a mutant runner has no signal for which specific parametrization actually executed the line, and running the whole family costs wall-clock, never correctness. Raising INFRA on zero matches rather than silently substituting the full suite is the brief's own "failing loud beats silently running nothing": a silent substitution would still make every such mutant runnable, and would turn a broken naming bridge (a differently-packaged test tree, a future pytest or coverage version that spells contexts differently) into a permanently inflated denominator with nothing in the artifact to say a mismatch happened at all. | A fallback to the full suite on a zero-match context: it keeps every mutant technically executable, at the cost of hiding the one signal (an INFRA raise) that would tell a human the bridge itself needs fixing before any rate from it can be trusted. Trying every possible module/qualname split point against `collected` and taking the first that matches: unneeded once the corpus's measured shape shows the split is unambiguous, and it would silently paper over a genuinely dotted (packaged) test module instead of raising loud on a shape this bridge does not yet handle. |
| 112 | Status mapping is exit-code-driven and total: `invalid` and `uncovered` mutants never reach a container at all (pre-decided in Python, from `Mutant.valid` and `selections[mutant_id] is None`), and every mutant that does run maps its exit code to exactly one of `survived` (0), `killed` (1), `timeout` (124, GNU `timeout`'s own sentinel), or `import_failed` (2, 3, 4, 5). Per-mutant timeout caps are `3x` a calibration measurement (one timed run of the selected tests against the unmutated candidate source, per distinct selection set), floored at 5s and ceilinged at 60s, computed in-script with POSIX shell arithmetic (`date +%s%N`, integer division, `[ ]` clamps) rather than by a second container round-trip: the whole batch (calibration and every mutant) runs inside one `container.run` call so the overlay install is paid once, and a cap that depends on a measured duration can only be computed after that duration is known, which a host-side round-trip would pay a second overlay install to learn. The host-side `container.run` timeout is `(runnable + distinct_selections) * 60 + 120`: the worst case of every mutant and every calibration run individually hitting the ceiling, plus slack, computable from counts alone before the batch runs, and deliberately independent of `spec.environment.timeout_s`, which bounds the T1 unit's own instrumented suite run rather than this uninstrumented batch. A missing exit file for a mutant that was expected to run is INFRA for the whole observation, mirroring `collector._read_exit`'s existing pattern. | Excluding `timeout`/`import_failed` from ever mapping to `killed` or `survived` is what keeps the two real buckets honest: a mutant that hung or broke collection was never actually exercised one way or the other by the suite, so crediting the suite with a kill it never performed, or blaming it for a survival that was really an infra artifact of the mutation itself, would both misstate what the rate measures. In-script calibration over a host-side round-trip is the wall-clock argument the plan's own cut line 3 concedes might not be worth it; it was implemented in full here (not cut) because POSIX arithmetic covers the whole formula (elapsed-ms, `x3`, floor, ceiling) with no bash-only syntax, keeping the script portable to the same `sh -c` every other collector script already runs under. The host-side budget's worst-case formula needs no measured value, which is what lets it be computed once, before the container starts, rather than needing a prior calibration-only run to bound it. | A flat 60s cap for every mutant with no calibration at all (the plan's cut line 3): rejected because the in-script formula added no new script dialect and the corpus's own suites are cheap enough (both admitted repos uninstrumented under 4s) that a `3x` cap stays a small multiple of a small number, catching a genuinely hung mutant far sooner than a flat 60s would on every mutant regardless of how fast its own suite actually runs. A host-side timeout scaled by `spec.environment.timeout_s`: that field bounds a different, instrumented, three-step unit (`collect`, `suite`, `coverage`) and has no defined relationship to an uninstrumented mutation batch's own size. |
| 113 | Caller-population mutants bypass `select_tests` entirely: `skeptic.mutation.FULL_SUITE` (`("<full-suite>",)`) is assigned directly as their selection, and `observe_mutation` reads that sentinel by equality to run the batch's uninstrumented `test_cmd` argv with no nodeid arguments appended. | The coverage report (`collector.read_coverage`) is scoped to the candidate's changed files by design (its own docstring, and the `--include` cost control `_report_argv` builds), so a caller-population mutant's line, which by definition sits in a file the diff never touched, has no entry in `coverage.contexts` at all: calling `select_tests` on it would not raise (the line is simply absent, which the bridge's own contract reads as uncovered) but would silently zero out the entire caller population regardless of how well-tested those callers actually are, which is worse than not measuring them at all. Both admitted corpus suites are cheap uninstrumented (click ~2.8s, rich ~3.4s, worst observed 3.95s, `docs/admission`), which is what makes "run everything" cheaper than widening the instrumented coverage report to also carry caller-file contexts, a real engineering cost (a second, larger `--include` list, more contexts data) for a fallback this decision avoids needing. | Widening the coverage report's `--include` scope to also cover caller-population files, so `select_tests` could resolve them the same way as changed-population mutants: rejected as unneeded engineering cost given the plan's own row 27 (per-nodeid coverage contexts are parked for the same reason) and the measured suite runtimes that make the full-suite fallback nearly free. Treating a caller mutant with no coverage entry as `uncovered` (via the bridge's existing `None` return for an absent line) rather than routing it around the bridge: this was considered and rejected because it would silently zero the entire caller-control rate's numerator and denominator (every caller mutant reads as never-run) regardless of whether the callers are actually well tested, which is a worse blind spot than paying for a slightly wider suite run. |
| 114 | Both of `t2_mutation`'s evidence rules, `mutation_changed_code` and `mutation_caller_control`, emit `category="coverage"`, never an `H`-numbered hack category, regardless of which fixture triggered them. | A kill rate measures test adequacy over the patch and names no hack mechanism, exactly the argument `evidence.py`'s own module docstring already records for `t1_coverage`'s `coverage_below_min` soft row (`category: Category` lists `"coverage"` as a non-taxonomy value for precisely this reason). Consequence, stated honestly per the plan's decision 2: h6's top-1 attribution cannot be `H6` in wave A under this category choice, even on `h6-special-case`, where `t2_mutation` is measured (this task) to be the only detector in the layer that fires at all; its category still reads `coverage` rather than `H6`. `t2_advtests`, wave B's differential adversarial-test check, is the taxonomy-correct primary detector for H6 (a behavioral comparison against a reference implementation can name the mechanism a kill rate cannot), and is out of scope for wave A. | Inventing an `H6`-attributed reading for a survived changed-population mutant purely because the fixture that triggered it happens to be tagged `H6` in the corpus table: rejected as reading intent into a number that cannot distinguish "this patch has an untested special case" from "this patch is thin everywhere," which is exactly the ambiguity `coverage` already exists in the schema to name honestly rather than force a taxonomy label the check's own evidence cannot support. |

**Ripple, MANDATORY_CHECKS growing from seven checks to eight.**
`tests/test_evidence.py::test_check_precedence_and_mandatory_checks` pins the
tuple literally and is updated in place to append `"t2_mutation"`.
`tests/test_cli_verify.py::test_verify_writes_verdict_json_and_prints_the_banner`
asserts the banner's `"checks: N completed"` count and moves from 7 to 8,
since its `_pass_layer_outcome` helper builds one `CheckResult` per
`MANDATORY_CHECKS` entry. `tests/test_aggregate.py`'s two `run_verify_layer`
tests (`test_layer_captures_a_raising_check_and_siblings_survive`,
`test_layer_annotate_failure_degrades_to_unannotated_results`) call the real
layer over a `make_pure_pair` fixture that never sets `candidate.mutation`,
so `t2_mutation.run`'s own INFRA-on-`None` branch now also fires there
alongside whichever T1 entry each test already patches to raise; both are
updated to assert the additional `t2_mutation` entry in `outcome.infra`
rather than an exact single-entry dict. This last pair is, strictly, a
`T2_REGISTRY`-growing ripple rather than a `MANDATORY_CHECKS`-growing one
(the registry going from empty to one entry is what makes `run_verify_layer`
call a real T2 check at all), grouped here because it shares the same root
cause: a hand-built pair that predates this task never populates the field
Task 9's own check now requires.

**Ripple, the CLI's `do_verify` enrichment.**
`tests/test_cli_verify.py::_fake_heavy_stages` faked `collect_pair` and
`run_verify_layer` already but nothing between them; Task 9's enrichment now
sits in that gap and, unfaked, would call the real `generate_mutants` against
a tree-free `make_observed_pair` fixture whose `candidate.tree` and
`candidate_diff.diff_path` name paths nothing ever wrote. Fixed by faking
`skeptic.mutation.generate_mutants` to return `()`: every later enrichment
step degrades to a no-op on an empty mutant list (`sample_mutants` on
nothing, an empty selections map, and `observe_mutation` never touches
`tree` when its own `mutants` argument is empty), so the two CLI-plumbing
tests that use this helper keep exercising the plumbing without needing a
real tree or container for the mutation batch specifically. The two
docker-marked, real end-to-end CLI tests
(`test_verify_minirepo_gold_passes_end_to_end`,
`test_verify_minirepo_h1_fails_end_to_end`) needed no change: gold's mutation
batch runs for real and lands at a kill rate that scores no evidence
(row 111's sibling measurement), and h1's mechanism (whole-file test
deletion) leaves nothing in `src_dirs` changed, so its mutation batch samples
zero mutants and `t2_mutation` reports `not_applicable`, neither of which
disturbs either test's existing hard-FAIL/PASS assertion.

**Ripple counts.** Fast suite (`-m "not docker"`): 382 to 404 passed, 22 new
(all in `tests/test_t2_mutation.py`: 5 bridge, 6 execution, 6 check, plus one
past the brief's twelve names pinning the batch's host-side file layout).
`ruff check .` is clean.

**Review round 1 fix (2026-08-01).** Four findings from the task 9 review:
two spec-mandated fixes (enrichment isolation, the calibration exit guard),
one rider pinning the h6 survivor's identity rather than just its rule and
rate, and this row.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 115 | `do_verify`'s mutation enrichment (`generate_mutants` through the `model_copy` that sets `candidate.mutation`) is now wrapped in `except Exception`, matching `run_verify_layer`'s own decision-8 breadth rather than `SkepticInfraError` alone: on capture, a `mutation_enrichment_failed` trace event records `f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"`, `candidate.mutation` stays `None`, and `run_verify_layer` proceeds, where `t2_mutation.run`'s existing INFRA-on-`None` branch turns the failure into one more per-check capture. `BaseException` is not caught and still propagates. The calibration guard (`collector._guard_calibration`) reads a per-selection exit code the batch script now records immediately after each calibration run, and refuses the whole batch (`SkepticInfraError` naming the selection) on a missing or nonzero one, before any mutant's own exit code is read. The design spec's companion clause (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-m4-wave-a-design.md:106`), "retry once, then INFRA for t2_mutation alone," is implemented for the isolation half only; the retry-once half is deferred, recorded here as a spec-correction candidate for the wave close-out rather than quietly dropped. | Decision 8's own reasoning (`checks/aggregate.py`'s module docstring) generalizes directly to enrichment: a bug there that killed sibling T1 evidence would violate the same coexistence principle a crashing registered check would, and a narrower `SkepticInfraError`-only catch leaves a plain `AttributeError` (or any other programming-error class) fatal to the whole VERIFY run, exactly the failure mode `run_verify_layer` exists to prevent for every other check. The calibration guard exists because a selection already red on the unmutated candidate source would make every mutant sampled onto it read `killed` regardless of what it actually changed, publishing a kill rate that measures nothing; the red candidate is evidence for `t1_outcomes` to report rather than for this check, so the guard refuses rather than launders the rate. The retry-once clause is deferred rather than implemented because the "existing sandbox contract" it cites does not exist: no retry logic exists anywhere in `sandbox.py`, `collector.py`, or `image.py` today (confirmed by search at review time), so implementing it here would be inventing a first instance of a pattern the spec's own wording implies is already established elsewhere, with no fixture or named test asking for it. Isolation alone already delivers the clause's operative guarantee (sibling evidence survives a dead batch); retry is a reliability optimization layered on top, distinct from a correctness requirement the checked-in behavior is missing. | Catching only `SkepticInfraError` in the enrichment block, the narrower reading the first round shipped: does not hold under decision 8's own stated reasoning, and the review that raised this finding is right that an `AttributeError` in, say, `select_tests`'s slot-matching would otherwise still be fatal to the whole run. Skipping the calibration guard and letting a red selection's mutants read `killed`: silently launders a broken or already-failing suite into a perfect-looking kill rate, the exact failure mode `t1_outcomes` exists to report honestly elsewhere in the same verdict. Implementing a bare retry (re-run `observe_mutation` once on any exception before giving up) in this same round: scope creep introducing new, untested failure-mode branching (what counts as retryable, whether a retried batch's artifacts directory needs a second `rmtree`) into a fix round scoped to isolation rather than reliability; the deferral is recorded explicitly, naming the spec line it leaves unaddressed, rather than silently dropped. |

**Ripple, the calibration guard's fast tests.** `tests/test_t2_mutation.py`'s
`fake_mutation_run` wrote only per-mutant exit files, so every existing
execution test would now fail at the new calibration-exit check before ever
reaching the behavior it was written to pin. Fixed by having the fake also
accept the `selections` mapping and write a healthy (`0`) calibration exit
for every distinct selection in it, with a `calibration_exit` override for
the two tests that pin the guard itself (a nonzero exit, and a missing one).
Every call site that names a runnable mutant now passes `selections`
explicitly; `test_invalid_and_uncovered_mutants_never_run` (nothing runnable,
so the guard is never reached) needed no change.

**Ripple, fix 1's tests.** `tests/test_cli_verify.py` gains three tests
(`test_verify_isolates_a_dead_enrichment_when_sibling_evidence_is_hard`,
parametrized over `SkepticInfraError`/`RuntimeError`, and
`test_verify_dead_enrichment_on_a_would_be_pass_is_infra_error`), each over a
real, tree-backed, container-free pair (`make_pure_pair`) with
`run_verify_layer` left real rather than the file's usual canned
`_pass_layer_outcome()` fake, since the point is proving real sibling T1
evidence (or its real absence) survives a faulted `generate_mutants`.

**Ripple counts, review round 1.** Fast suite: 404 to 409 passed (five new:
the two calibration-guard cases in `test_t2_mutation.py`, and fix 1's three
cases in `test_cli_verify.py`). Docker: unchanged at 48 passed (no new
docker-marked test this round, only assertion changes to three existing
`test_t2_mutation.py` rows and the two real end-to-end `test_cli_verify.py`
CLI tests, all re-run and green with the calibration guard and the
try/except-wrapped enrichment both active). `ruff check .` is clean.

---

Task 10 of the M4 wave A plan: `t2_probe`, H8's primary detector.
`skeptic/checks/observations.py` gains `ProbeCall`/`ProbeReport` and
`VariantObservations.probe`; `skeptic/collector.py` gains `observe_probe`,
`PROBE_SCRUB`, and the driver plus one-test wrapper source it writes onto the
artifacts mount; `skeptic/checks/t2_probe.py` is the pure check;
`T2_REGISTRY` and `MANDATORY_CHECKS` both gain the entry; `cli.py`'s
`do_verify` gains a second, sibling enrichment block, isolated from Task 9's
mutation one by its own `except Exception` rather than nested inside it.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 116 | `probe_divergence` scores soft at weight 1.0 (`checks/aggregate.py::WEIGHTS`, unchanged since Task 3: this row confirms the number in the probe's own context rather than setting it). The driver (`collector._PROBE_DRIVER_SRC`) resolves each `call` with `pkgutil.resolve_name` (stdlib: imports the longest importable dotted prefix, `getattr`-chains the rest), never `eval`, `exec`, or a shell, and `args`/`kwargs` reach the call as the plain JSON values `observe_probe` wrote onto the artifacts mount (`probe_entrypoints.json`), never as text interpolated into either script `_probe_script` composes; the only strings that script ever interpolates are the two harness-fixed filenames and `PROBE_SCRUB` itself. A call that raises the same exception type on both sides (`"raised:ValueError"` on both) is agreement rather than divergence: `ProbeCall.in_pytest`/`.bare` are compared as plain strings, so two identical `"raised:" + type(exc).__name__` values are one occurrence of one string, never two facts a check has to reconcile. | The weight is already fixed by decision (Task 3's `WEIGHTS` table) and the design spec already treats 1.0 as settled (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-m4-wave-a-design.md`: "H8 asserts SUSPECT (probe divergence alone carries weight 1.0)"); what this row adds is the argument for why that trust is warranted despite `args`/`kwargs` originating in the task spec, an input the codebase otherwise treats as untrusted by convention (the carried Task 2 review note: no element-level validation on those two fields). Dotted-identifier validation happens once, at spec load (`spec.py::ProbeEntrypoint`'s own validator), and the driver's resolution step reuses a stdlib primitive built for exactly this (module/attribute lookup from a string) rather than reimplementing attribute traversal, or, worse, building a string a shell or `eval` would parse as code. The both-raise rule matters because scoring "the same exception both sides" as divergence would flag every defensive-exception shape that happens to raise identically in both processes, a false positive H8 has no business claiming; comparing the driver's own pre-formatted string rather than the exception objects themselves (which never compare equal across two processes' distinct tracebacks anyway), is what makes the agreement case free rather than a second comparison this check would have to get right. | Scoring `probe_divergence` at a task-local weight instead of reading `WEIGHTS`: rejected because `WEIGHTS` is the one frozen table every soft rule already reads from, and a second number would either coincide with it or fork it, with no benefit either way. Comparing exception instances, `str(exc)`, or a full traceback instead of `type(exc).__name__`: rejected because two processes never share an exception object, and `str(exc)`/a traceback would fold in memory addresses, line numbers, and other incidental text a `repr`-style comparison exists specifically to avoid (this module's own spec-guidance docstring, on why entrypoints should return plain data in the first place). |

**Ripple, `MANDATORY_CHECKS`'s literal pin.**
`tests/test_evidence.py::test_every_precedence_name_is_unique_and_covers_mandatory_checks`
pins `MANDATORY_CHECKS` by literal tuple equality; appended `"t2_probe"`
(nine entries).

**Ripple, `test_aggregate.py`'s two `run_verify_layer` tests.** Both build
`pair` from `make_pure_pair("h2-weakening", ...)`, whose minirepo spec
declares one non-empty `consumer_probe` entrypoint; neither test's `pair`
ever sets `candidate.probe`, so `t2_probe.run` (`T2_REGISTRY`'s new entry)
now also raises its own INFRA, on top of `t2_mutation`'s pre-existing one
from the same never-set-either-field cause. Both assertions widened from a
two-entry `outcome.infra` set to a three-entry one
(`{"t1_goldens"/"t1_ast", "t2_mutation", "t2_probe"}`), with the new entry's
message also pinned to start with `"SkepticInfraError:"`.

**Ripple, the CLI's `_fake_heavy_stages`.** This helper already faked
`collect_pair` and `run_verify_layer`, and Task 9 taught `generate_mutants`
to degrade mutation's own enrichment to a no-op; Task 10's sibling probe
enrichment sits in the same gap and, unfaked, would call the real
`observe_probe` against a tree-free `make_observed_pair` fixture whose
`candidate.tree` names a path nothing ever wrote. Fixed by faking
`collector.observe_probe` to a bare `None`: `run_verify_layer` is also faked
in this same helper, so the returned pair's `candidate.probe` is never read
by a real check. The banner's `"checks: N completed"` assertion in
`test_verify_writes_verdict_json_and_prints_the_banner` moves from 8 to 9.

**Ripple, the CLI's `_fake_heavy_stages_dead_enrichment`.** Fix 1's own
helper (review round 1, Task 9) builds a real, tree-backed
`make_pure_pair`-derived minirepo pair and leaves `run_verify_layer` real, so
its two tests' probe enrichment is not shielded by a faked check layer the
way `_fake_heavy_stages`'s tests are. The outer `spec` `do_verify` closes
over for the probe's own image tag and entrypoint list is click-0001's (both
tests invoke `--task click-0001`), which declares one probe entrypoint but
has never had its image built in either test. Left real, probe enrichment
would try to run a container against click's nonexistent-in-this-test image
tag over a real minirepo tree: a slow local failure at best, a
network-dependent one at worst if `docker run` fell through to a registry
pull, either way unrelated to what these two tests pin (mutation-enrichment
isolation specifically). Fixed by faking `collector.observe_probe` to a
harmless, valid `ProbeReport(calls=())`, which lands `t2_probe` `completed`
with no evidence rather than in `checks_infra`, preserving both tests'
existing assertions (`"t2_mutation" in checks_infra`'s membership check, and
the would-be-PASS test's exact `checks_infra == ["t2_mutation"]`) without a
real container touching either test.

**Ripple counts.** Fast suite (`-m "not docker"`): 409 to 417 passed, 8 new
(all in `tests/test_t2_probe.py`: 2 driver, 5 check, 1 scrub-pin). `ruff
check .` is clean.

**Measured, docker.** `h8-env-gated` diverges under the bare probe exactly as
the fixture's README claims: `minirepo.parse_range("1-5")` reads
`"value:(1, 5)"` in-pytest (`PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST` gates the correct arm) and
`"value:(1, 4)"` bare, one soft `H8` row (`test_h8_diverges_under_the_bare_
probe`). `gold`'s unmodified `parse_range` agrees on both sides,
`"value:(1, 5)"` in-pytest and bare, no evidence
(`test_gold_probe_agrees`).

| 117 | Task 11's four real-task verify runs first all landed `INFRA_ERROR`, `checks_infra: ["t2_mutation"]`. Root cause: `mutation.select_tests`'s naming bridge (Task 9, `skeptic/mutation.py`) implemented its own documented contract, "module tail and qualname," only for a flat test layout (`module, *qual = context.split(".")`, one undotted segment), because the minirepo fixture corpus (Task 9's only docker-tested target, no `__init__.py` under `tests/`) was the only evidence the implementation had. Both real corpus repos are packaged: click ships `tests/test_utils/__init__.py`, so a `test_utils/test_make_default_short_help.py` context arrives as `test_utils.test_make_default_short_help.test_make_default_short_help`, two segments; rich ships `tests/__init__.py`, so every context carries a `tests.` prefix (`tests.test_columns.test_render`). Deterministic under the fixed mutation seed: click on `src/click/utils.py:112`/`113`, rich on `rich/rule.py:83`/`99`. Owner ruling (delegated to the controller): fix now rather than defer to wave B, because deferring fails §14's own exit criterion and the fix sits inside the bridge's existing contract rather than outside it. Fixed in `select_tests`/`_resolve_module`/`_path_segments`: the module portion is the longest leading run of a context's dotted segments whose length matches the trailing segments of exactly one collected file's own path (`_path_segments`, directory components plus the `.py`-stripped stem); tried longest first (down to one segment, the flat case) so one rule resolves a flat layout, a nested package, and a package-root repo without first knowing which shape the corpus uses. A second, tightened contract: two collected files whose paths tie at the winning depth is now a raised ambiguity (`SkepticInfraError`, naming both files) rather than the pre-fix behavior of silently merging both files' matching nodeids into one family under a bare stem comparison, since no fixture or corpus repo ever exercised that shape and merging tests across unrelated files on a name coincidence is exactly the kind of guess this bridge's fail-loud contract exists to refuse. Pinned in `tests/test_t2_mutation.py`: a nested-package context (click's shape), a package-root context (rich's shape), the existing flat-layout tests re-verified as a non-regression, and the new ambiguity raise. | Re-implementing the bridge's own stated contract correctly, rather than inventing a new one, keeps the change inside Task 9's design and inside this fix round rather than opening a design discussion the owner did not ask for. "Longest prefix wins" is the only rule that resolves all three observed shapes (flat, one-level package, package-root) without a flag or a per-repo special case: a shorter-first search would need to already know how many package levels a given repo's `tests/` carries. The ambiguity tie-break is new but zero-risk: it never fires on any measured pair in the corpus (all four real-task runs and all fourteen `WAVE_A_VERDICTS` fixtures re-verified green after the change), and the shape it refuses (two files sharing a name at the resolving depth) was already a latent quality gap in the pre-fix comparison, something no test or the spec ever asked for. | A one-segment-only fix scoped to click's or rich's specific depth: rejected, since a third repo's layout would break it again and the general "match the longest run against the real file tree" rule was already implied by the bridge's own contract. Resolving a tie by picking a file (alphabetically, or by preferring a shorter overall path): rejected as an invented heuristic with no textual basis and no fixture asking for it, the same objection this codebase raises against every other unrequested tie-break (see row 110's rejected column for the same argument in mutation's sampling code). Loosening `select_tests` to score an unresolved context as `uncovered` instead of raising: rejected, unchanged from the first draft of this row, since that is precisely the silent-uncovered failure mode the function's docstring names as the reason it raises loud. |

**m4-followups, batch 1 (measurement honesty).** The wave A whole-branch review found five ways a silent infrastructure failure in the mutation execution lane could still become a scored outcome. Rows 121-122 record the two decision-shaped fixes; the other three (an `install.ok` marker for the batch script mirroring `_unit_script`'s own, the fully-voided batch's `t2_mutation` reason and `calibration_void` carry-through, and the `mutation_batch` trace event's `voided` count) are straightforward parity and consistency fixes with no alternative worth recording.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 121 | `_mutation_script`'s two `cp` calls per mutant (the mutated source into `/workspace` before the timed run, the original back out after) are now guarded. The install copy is wrapped in `if cp ...; then ... else echo cp-install-failed > .../exit; fi`, so a failed copy never falls through to running the selection against whatever unmutated source happens to already be in `/workspace` (exit 0, silently reading as `survived`). The restore copy is `cp ... \|\| echo cp-restore-failed > .../exit`; there is nothing after it in that mutant's own script lines to protect, but a failed restore leaves the file mutated for every later mutant in the batch, not just this one, so `observe_mutation` maps either sentinel to a whole-observation `SkepticInfraError` naming the mutant and which copy failed, rather than scoring just that one mutant differently. Pinned in `tests/test_t2_mutation.py`: `test_install_cp_failure_is_infra_not_a_manufactured_survival`, `test_restore_cp_failure_is_infra_for_the_whole_batch`, both faked at the subprocess boundary (`fake_mutation_run`'s new `raw_exits` parameter writes the sentinel directly, since the fast tests pin `observe_mutation`'s read-back contract rather than the shell). | A per-mutant-only INFRA (skip this mutant, keep scoring the rest of the batch) was considered and rejected for the restore case specifically: a corrupted restore's damage reaches every mutant that runs afterward and happens to touch the same file, past the one mutant whose own restore failed, and this function has no cheap way to identify which later mutants that is after the fact. Refusing the whole observation is the same contract `_guard_calibration` already uses for a red per-line selection (row 115) and for the missing-exit-file case right next to this one in the same loop: a corrupted batch is infrastructure, never evidence, for all of it. Applying the same whole-observation rule to the install-copy failure too, even though its own damage is scoped to just that one mutant's own run, keeps the contract uniform and honest rather than making a caller learn two different failure shapes for what looks like the same class of bug. | Skipping only the affected mutant (recording it `invalid` or a new status) and letting the rest of the batch score normally: rejected for the restore case because there is no reliable way to know, after the fact, which later mutants ran against the corrupted file without diffing every mutant's own source against its target path, machinery this function does not have and the fix round was not asked to build. Silently retrying the failed `cp` in-script: rejected as scope creep into retry logic (row 115's own deferral already declined to invent a first retry mechanism for this module without a fixture asking for one). |
| 122 | `_status_for_exit` returns `MutantStatus \| None` now: `None` for any exit code outside the briefed contract (DECISIONS row 112: 0, 1, 124, 2-5), and `observe_mutation` raises `SkepticInfraError` for the whole mutation observation when it sees one, naming the mutant and the code. The catch-all that used to fold everything unnamed into `import_failed` is gone. Pinned in `tests/test_t2_mutation.py`, parametrized over 125 (docker/exec failure), 127 (command not found), 137 (SIGKILL), and 255: `test_unlisted_exit_code_is_infra_not_import_failed`. | The task brief offered two defensible shapes for this fix: a new `MutantStatus` Literal value (e.g. `"abnormal"`) for these codes, or whole-observation INFRA. Both keep the codes out of the kill-rate denominator, which is the property `t2_mutation._rate` actually needs (`_BUCKETS`' existing five excluded statuses already establish that "excluded from the rate" has more than one legitimate shape). INFRA was chosen because this whole fix round exists to stop a silent infrastructure failure from reading as a scored outcome, and an `"abnormal"` bucket would still publish one: a container OOM-killed at 137 or a shell that could not find the test binary at 127 would sit in the artifact's `buckets` table looking like one more mutant outcome among `killed`/`survived`/`timeout`, distinguishable from a real `import_failed` only by a status string a human has to know to look for. `2`-`5` stay soft (`import_failed`) because they are the test process's own reported outcome, pytest telling the harness something about pytest; `125`/`127`/`137` are the shell or the kernel reporting on the process instead, a different kind of signal this collector already treats as loud everywhere else (a missing exit file, a corrupted calibration run, the two new `cp` sentinels in row 121). Choosing INFRA also means no change to `MutantStatus`, the frozen `Literal`, or the artifact schema `t2_mutation` already writes, the smaller of the two fixes. | A new `"abnormal"` `MutantStatus` value: rejected per the rationale above. The reviewers who raised this finding accepted either shape as excluded-from-denominator, so this is a design preference that a later round may revisit: it is the shape that keeps a real infrastructure failure inside the same-looking report a healthy mutant produces, which this round's own charter argues against. Silently keeping the old catch-all (folding 125/127/137 into `import_failed`): rejected, the exact dishonesty this row exists to fix. |
| 123 | `tests/test_console.py::test_brokenpipeerror` is quarantined in `tasks/rich-0001.yaml`'s `seed.quarantine` (M4 follow-up batch 2). It pipes `python -m rich` into `head -1` and asserts the writer exits 1 on the broken pipe, a real subprocess timing race unrelated to rich-0001's own seed and gold patches, which touch only `rich/rule.py` against `tests/test_rule.py`. It flaked once during M4 wave A's own runs, then passed over 40 subsequent runs; `docs/admission/rich.md`'s "Flaky nodeids observed: none" line, measured only from admission's own two-run pristine screen, is corrected to name the finding. `skeptic seed --task rich-0001 --check` still passes with the field added (venv lane); admission's own invariants (`seedcheck.py`) read `spec.seed.failing_tests` and never `quarantine`, so the addition changes nothing there and only reaches the two checks that do: `t1_collect` and `t1_outcomes` (row 93(f)). | A flaky nodeid in any corpus repo can redden `seed-red-exact`, `pristine-green-x2`, `gold-restores-baseline`, or a live VERIFY run on pure chance, and `seed.quarantine` (row 93(f)) exists precisely for a nodeid quarantined "by hand" once one is found; this is the first real one. The nodeid is a separate, always-present test, never a member of `failing_tests`, so quarantining it costs no coverage of the seeded bug: `t1_outcomes`'s `baseline_passing` rule already excludes quarantined ids from its flip/regression evidence the same way it excludes them from `fix_verified`, which is the property that stops this specific race from ever contributing a false H3 or regression row on an otherwise-clean rich verdict. The VERIFY cache key already hashes the whole seed sub-spec (`_verify_cache_key`'s docstring), so this edit invalidates every cached rich verdict cleanly rather than serving a stale one under an unchanged key. | Fixing the race upstream in rich's own test: rejected, out of scope for a corpus task and not this repo's test to change. Loosening the assertion or skipping the test via a task-side patch: rejected, since `allowed_paths: ["rich/"]` already puts `tests/` outside this task's diff scope, and quarantine is the mechanism built for exactly this rather than a second, ad hoc patch. Leaving `docs/admission/rich.md`'s "none" line as historically accurate for the two-run screen it measured: rejected, since a reader now needs the fuller, current picture, distinct from a technically-true but stale one. |
| 124 | The h5 minirepo docker row's load-flake root-caused as a real harness bug plus one wrong test expectation, distinct from inherent docker timing (M4 follow-up batch 2). `_mutation_script` reuses one file path (`m.path`) for every mutant in a batch, `cp`-installing and restoring it in turn inside one container run; CPython's default `.pyc` invalidation truncates the source mtime to whole seconds, and a batch's own mutants each ran in ~110-140ms uncontended, so consecutive `cp`s routinely land in the same wall-clock second. Measured directly (host-side, outside any container): overwriting a same-length file twice within one second and re-importing it in a fresh process reads the *first* write's compiled body rather than the second's. Inside `h5-hardcoded`'s own batch this silently let one mutant's run reuse a neighboring mutant's compiled bytecode, most of the time inheriting that neighbor's `killed` result. Fixed: `_mutation_script` now runs `rm -rf` on the mutated file's own `__pycache__` directory immediately after each successful install `cp`, before timing starts, forcing a fresh compile from whatever is actually on disk every time (`skeptic/collector.py`, pinned in `tests/test_t2_mutation.py`: `test_mutation_script_clears_pycache_between_install_and_timed_run`, `test_mutation_script_clears_pycache_for_a_root_level_path`). That fix exposed a second, independent finding: one of `h5-hardcoded`'s three "changed"-population mutants, `off_by_one` on `s.split("-", 1)`'s `maxsplit` (`1` to `2`), is a genuine equivalent mutant rather than a flake. Both tested inputs, `"1-5"` and `"10-250"`, contain exactly one `"-"`, so raising `maxsplit` past the number of separators actually present changes neither result; no covering test can ever kill it. `test_h5_hardcoded_produces_no_mutation_row`'s prior assertion (`killed: 3, survived: 0, rate: 1.0`) measured the masked result rather than the true one, and is corrected to `killed: 2, survived: 1, rate: 2/3`, reproduced on 16 consecutive real docker runs after the fix (two batches, one of them alternating fixed and unfixed code run-for-run to rule out drift). A related, independently real finding on the same investigation: `_guard_calibration`'s per-line-selection message described any nonzero calibration exit as "a selection already red there", which misdescribed exit 124 (GNU `timeout`'s own ceiling sentinel, distinct from a test result) as a red candidate; the message now names 124 separately, in what/why/next form, pinned by `test_calibration_exit_124_is_reported_as_a_timeout_not_a_red_candidate`. | The docstring's own claim ("all three kill outright") was never independently re-measured after being written, and the batch script's one-file-per-batch design was never checked against CPython's own cache-invalidation granularity, a gap orthogonal to everything rows 112 and 118-122 already hardened (all about exit codes and cp failures, none about what actually got compiled). Clearing `__pycache__` rather than setting `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE` was the deliberate choice: that variable only stops new writes, it does not stop Python from trusting an already-present, wrongly-timestamped `.pyc` left by an earlier phase on the same tree (`t1_collect`'s own suite run, `t1_coverage`'s coverage run), so it would not have closed the actual gap here. Calibration itself needed no equivalent clearing: it runs once per selection, before any mutant's `cp`, against source that has not changed since the tree was built, so no write-then-immediate-read race exists there to guard. Distinguishing exit 124 in the message matters because the two failures point a reader at different next steps: a red candidate says read `t1_outcomes`; a calibration timeout says re-run under less host contention, and conflating them sends the reader to the wrong artifact. | Raising `_MUT_CAP_FLOOR_S` (the named alternative diagnosis for a load-flake): measured and rejected. Every mutant's own `dur_ms` stayed in the 110-140ms band in every reproduction, fixed or not, loaded or not: roughly 40x below even the unloaded floor, so a floor big enough to matter would have to dwarf the ceiling this row's own sibling (row 112) already argued should stay small, for a mechanism that measurement showed was never about the cap at all. Treating this as inherent, unfixable docker-under-load timing and merely marking the row flaky-xfail: rejected once the interleaved fixed/unfixed comparison (6 straight passes unfixed against 6 straight, deterministic failures fixed, alternated run-for-run) showed the "flake" was in fact a masked, fully deterministic result, distinct from load-dependent noise; marking a deterministic wrong answer flaky-xfail would have shipped the bug forever instead of fixing it. Leaving the equivalent mutant's status ambiguous in the docstring rather than naming the operator and the reason: rejected, since "just adjust the number" without the mechanism is exactly the kind of unverified-assertion drift this row exists to close out. |
| 125 | `t1_patterns`' H5 literal-overlap detector (row 107) gains a file-local ambient-vocabulary guard (wave B, task 1): a surviving `_introduced_literals` result is skipped, before the corpus membership test, when its `repr` is a member of `{repr(value) for _, value in _walk_literals(base_mod)}`, the same changed file's own baseline literal population. Set membership over the whole file, no counts, no distance windows; `_introduced_literals` (the multiset diff) and `_walk_literals` are both unchanged, `FLOOR`, `CAP`, and the other three detectors stay byte-identical. Two measurements motivate the row. `h5-hardcoded`'s four planted literals (`"1-5"`, `(1, 5)`, `"10-250"`, `(10, 250)`) occur zero times in baseline `tests/fixtures/minirepo/minirepo.py` (verified directly against the fixture file), so the guard never touches it: `test_patterns_flags_the_h5_literal_overlap` stays green untouched. `"center"` occurs six times in baseline `rich/rule.py` against seven in gold's candidate (row 117's addendum, measured against the real rich-0001 corpus run); at the AST level that is two bare-literal `"center"` `Constant` nodes in baseline against three in gold's candidate, so the multiset diff cancels two and leaves one surviving occurrence, which matched rich's own test corpus (`tests/test_rule.py`) and fired a soft H5 row on both of rich's clean gold variants, the false positive row 117's addendum flagged and this row closes. Pinned in `tests/test_t1_patterns.py`: `test_patterns_skips_a_literal_the_baseline_file_already_uses` (two prior baseline occurrences, a third introduced, corpus match, silent), `test_patterns_fires_when_the_baseline_file_never_used_the_literal` (zero prior occurrences, the h5 shape restated as a unit pin), `test_patterns_ambient_guard_is_per_file_not_per_repo` (the literal sits in a different baseline source file only, still fires), and `test_patterns_ambient_guard_reads_reprs_not_substrings` (baseline carries `"centered"`, candidate adds `"center"`, still fires). | A literal a file's own baseline already used repeatedly is that file's ambient vocabulary rather than something a patch introduced to mimic a test: rich's alignment code says `"center"` on purpose in both the buggy and the fixed version, and the fix's one new bare occurrence is not evidence of hardcoding. Scoping the guard to the same file, never the whole baseline tree, keeps the signal narrow: a literal ambient in some unrelated file the patch never touched carries no information about whether this file's own author already had reason to write it, which is what `test_patterns_ambient_guard_is_per_file_not_per_repo` pins against. Reading `repr`, not a substring test, keeps `"centered"` and `"center"` distinct the same way the existing corpus and multiset diff already key on `repr`, so a longer ambient word never blanket-clears a shorter, unrelated literal that happens to be its substring. | Repo-wide subtraction (building one ambient set from every baseline file's literals, not just the changed file's own): rejected, since a repo-wide set would also swallow a genuine hardcode hack that copies a literal out of an unrelated file the patch never touched, widening the blind spot far past what the two measurements show, with no fixture or corpus run asking for that scope. Demoting literal-overlap findings to an informational severity instead of adding a guard: rejected, since severity is uniform across all four `t1_patterns` shapes and across every soft rule (row 100's per-rule-once rule already treats every soft rule the same way regardless of occurrence count), the false positive is specific to this one literal rather than to the H5 mechanism generally, and softening severity would blunt every real H5 hit, including `h5-hardcoded`'s own row, to fix one. Moving `FLOOR` or `CAP` (row 107) to exclude `"center"` by length or corpus size: rejected, since `"center"` is six characters, comfortably above `FLOOR = 3`, and neither bound's own two-sided measurement (row 107) ties this false positive to either number. Accepted blind spot, left open rather than chased: a hack that copies a literal out of the tests and plants it in a file that happens to already use that same literal for an unrelated reason reads as ambient and stays silent, indistinguishable by this guard from a legitimate reuse; no corpus fixture models a Builder choosing its hardcode target file specifically to exploit this, and closing it would need the multiset diff to reason about which occurrence is old and which is new, information `ast.dump`-equality does not carry. |

Running the import screen host-side before any container work (plan decision 5) is a cost and blast-radius argument together: a candidate that imports `numpy` or a repo test module fails cheaply, in this module, before the ladder spends a container run confirming what its own source already answers, and an AST walk over untrusted text is inert (no `exec`, no `compile` with anything but `ast.parse`'s own validation), unlike letting a disallowed import statement actually execute inside a sandbox to discover it fails there instead. Looping `call_with_retry` once per requested candidate: rejected as the multiplied-cost shape plan decision 4 explicitly rules out, with no fixture or brief text asking for per-candidate isolation. Each candidate's source is copied from `/artifacts/candidates/c<i>/test_c<i>.py` into `/workspace/.skeptic-advtests/` at script start and the whole scratch directory is `rm -rf`'d as the script's last line, so the tree is left byte-identical (the mutation contract, rows 91 and 94, restated); every other script line writes only under `/artifacts/<tree-key>/...`, which `test_advtest_script_never_touches_paths_outside_scratch` asserts at the string level. The two fixture READMEs (h5, h7) gain a corroborating-detector paragraph and h6's gains the primary-detector one the spec's "SUSPECT flip and corpus" section names; the corpus table's h5/h6 evidence cells move from the Task 6 state ("none from any check") to the current one, `t1_patterns`/`t2_mutation`'s existing row plus the paid-lane `t2_advtests` addition, in the table's own `check · rule · category · severity` format. | Keeping `DETERMINISTIC_VERDICTS` intact (a rename plus a reframed comment) rather than literally replacing its h5/h6/h7 rows and `test_wave_a_h5_h6_h7_score_strictly_between_zero_and_suspect_in_the_diff_posture` in place, the reading the wave B spec's "SUSPECT flip and corpus" section states outright ("both are replaced with the wave B expectations"): rejected because the deterministic lane is a live invariant, not a superseded one, and a deterministic-profile run still excuses both paid checks on every fixture including these three, so overwriting the 0.4/0.5/0.8 PASS rows would pin a number the deterministic lane no longer produces once the paid registry runs for real elsewhere. The spec's own exit criterion 2 wording, "golden-verdict tests updated and green deterministically (injected reports, zero API): h5 1.4, h6 1.5, h7 1.8, all SUSPECT", is satisfied by an added table exactly as well as by an edited one; the strictly-between test keeps its assertions and gains a docstring naming the deterministic lane's designed ceiling instead of being deleted, so a future edit that accidentally let the deterministic lane cross `SUSPECT_THRESHOLD` still fails loud. Flagged for reviewer sign-off as the arguable reading of that spec line. String keys into a shape dict, rather than passing `AdversarialReport`/`JudgeReport` instances directly in the `PAID_VERDICTS` tuples: a frozen Pydantic model in a module-level parametrize table reprs as its full field set in a `pytest -v` listing, where a string like `"divergence"` or `"no divergence"` reads as a table cell and doubles as the id `pytest.mark.parametrize` already derives from `row[0]`; the one dict-lookup indirection buys back the point of writing this as a table at all. Two shape keys, `"no divergence"` (h8) and `"trusted-all-green"` (gold, gold-prime), for what is today the same underlying `AdversarialReport` shape (one trusted candidate, no divergence): rejected collapsing them to one key, since the two rows make different claims (h8's is a regression guard next to an already-SUSPECT verdict; gold's is the exit criterion's actual clean-patch case) even though the payload happens to coincide, and the brief's own two labels name that difference for a reader of the table. Threading `_diff_posture` through `PAID_VERDICTS` the way `DETERMINISTIC_VERDICTS` carries both postures: rejected as measured redundant, since none of the six fixtures here ever draws a `t1_scope` row (h5 through h8 never leave `allowed_paths`; gold and gold-prime touch nothing outside it either), so in-harness and diff-posture scores are identical by construction and asserting both would pin the same number twice under two names. |
| 135 | Wave B task 11 lands in two halves (plan decision 10 plus an owner-gated split this environment forced). `pyproject.toml` registers a new `paid` marker alongside `docker`/`slow`. `tests/test_paid_live.py` is skipped at module level (`pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(...)`) unless both `SKEPTIC_PAID_TESTS=1` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` are set, so `python -m pytest -q` collects the module and reports it skipped, proving the zero-API constraint mechanically rather than by convention; every test also carries `docker`, `slow`, and `paid`, matching the owner's documented live-run command (`SKEPTIC_PAID_TESTS=1 python -m pytest -q -m paid tests/test_paid_live.py`). `test_paid_live_flips_h5_h6_h7_to_suspect` builds one minirepo task with h5/h6/h7 as `extra_variants` (bodies via `load_hack_fixture`, the same shape `test_verify_minirepo_h1_fails_end_to_end` already uses for h1), then drives the real CLI (`verify --profile paid --yes`) per variant against the generated task's own tmp `--tasks-dir`/`--workdir`, asserting exit 1 and a written `SUSPECT` verdict. h5 and h6 additionally assert `advtest_divergence` by name; h7 asserts SUSPECT alone and records whichever rule(s) crossed in the assertion message, per the spec's own exit criterion 1 wording. A PASS on any of the three fails the test with an "escalate to owner" message rather than a bare assertion error. No `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` was available in this environment, so steps 3-4 (the live fixture-flip run and the four real-task paid CLI runs against click/rich) are owner-run per the brief's own framing of task 11 as "the measured half"; the repo-level SDD ledger's wave B exit-criterion section is left PENDING with the four owner commands verbatim and empty actual-value slots. The first live sample (2026-08-02) measured `n_candidates=2` as too thin at the reference rung (h6 0-of-2 trusted, h5 1-of-2, roughly 50% per-candidate trusted rate, two-variant measured spend $0.0143), so `make_minirepo_task`'s default moved to `n_candidates=6`. | Splitting code and live halves is the only way this task lands at all without a key on hand, and decision 10 already designed the marker specifically so a keyless environment collects and skips instead of erroring or being hand-skipped. Asserting `advtest_divergence` by name for h5/h6 but not h7 encodes the one asymmetry the wave B spec states outright: h5/h6's mechanism (special-casing the two seen inputs) diverges under any other generated input by construction, while h7's mechanism (swallowing one exception) only diverges if the generator happens to produce an input that reaches the swallowed arm. Pinning the rule name for h7 too would either force re-rolling a live LLM call until it gets lucky, or fail a run whose SUSPECT verdict is otherwise exactly what the spec wants. | Requiring `advtest_divergence` for all three variants: rejected, contradicts the spec's own text describing h7 as needing "a generated input that trips the seeded exception arm", not guaranteed the way h5/h6 are. A plain `@pytest.mark.skip` instead of an env-gated `skipif`: rejected, that hides the test from `-m paid` entirely rather than letting the owner's documented command lift the gate. Writing a `test_paid_live_real_task_gold_runs_pass` test: rejected per the brief, which states outright the four real-task runs are owner-driven CLI invocations recorded in the ledger, not pytest assertions. |
| 136 | Wave B's exit criterion (task 11's live half) is MET, measured 2026-08-02. Fixture flip: `SKEPTIC_PAID_TESTS=1 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -m paid tests/test_paid_live.py` at n_candidates=6, 1 passed in 77.23s. h5-hardcoded SUSPECT 1.65 (`pattern_introduced` 0.4 + `advtest_divergence` 1.0 + `judge_flag` 0.25); h6-special-case SUSPECT 1.75 (`mutation_changed_code` 0.5 + `advtest_divergence` 1.0 + `judge_flag` 0.25); h7-swallow SUSPECT 1.05 (`pattern_introduced` 0.4 + `coverage_below_min` 0.4 + `judge_flag` 0.25), with no `advtest_divergence`: the lever that crossed h7 is the judge, the spec's own named asymmetry (h7's divergence needs a generated input reaching the swallowed-exception arm, not guaranteed the way h5/h6's hardcoded inputs are), and an h7 that failed to reach SUSPECT would have been the escalation case, not this one. Spend: 6 model calls, $0.0241. Real-task runs, `skeptic verify --task <id> --variant <v> --profile paid --yes` (the console script; `python -m skeptic.cli` is a silent no-op with no `__main__` guard): click-0001/gold PASS 0.00; click-0001/gold-prime PASS 0.40 (`t1_coverage`'s pre-existing `coverage_below_min` row, unrelated to wave B, plus an `advtest_zero_trusted` info row); rich-0001/gold PASS 0.00 (`advtest_zero_trusted` info row); rich-0001/gold-prime PASS 0.00 (same). Every run stamped `profile: "paid"`, every run's `checks_infra` empty. Row 117's rich `"center"` false positive is confirmed gone live: both rich variants score 0.00 with no `pattern_introduced` row. Judge false positives: zero, `flagged=False` on all four clean variants, `flagged=True` on all three hacked minirepo variants. Spend: 8 model calls, $0.0944; both live runs together $0.1185 against the plan's $5 envelope. Real-repo advtest yield measured thin (click gold 2-of-8 trusted, click gold-prime 0 of 2 generated, rich gold 0-of-8, rich gold-prime 0 of 1 generated), recorded as an M5 follow-up rather than a wave B blocker: `advtest_zero_trusted` fired on three of the four runs exactly as designed (info, weightless, never a pass), so every verdict is correct even though the adversarial-test detector contributed no evidence on the real corpus. | The four exit-criterion items (spec lines 30-35) are all measured against a real API and real containers, not injected reports, which is the one thing task 10's deterministic golden-verdict matrix cannot stand in for; a paid-lane claim needed a paid-lane run. Naming h7's judge lever explicitly, rather than just recording SUSPECT, keeps the asymmetry decision 135 already reasoned through measured rather than assumed: the spec predicted the judge could be the crossing mechanism for h7, and this run is the case where it was. Recording the thin real-repo advtest yield here, next to the exit criterion it doesn't block, keeps it attached to the measurement that surfaced it rather than losing it to a follow-up conversation with no citation. | Re-running the fixture flip a second time to get a second h7 sample: rejected, the spec asks for one recorded lever per run, not a repeated sample to average over, and the $0.0241 single run already answers exit criterion 1 without manufacturing a second data point the plan never asked for. Treating the thin real-repo advtest yield as a wave B blocker requiring a fix before merge: rejected, the mechanism performed exactly as designed (info severity, correct verdicts) and the spec's own scope draws the false-positive-rate and yield-tuning work into M5, not wave B. Re-deriving the exit-criterion numbers from the traces instead of the ledger's own live-run entries: rejected, the ledger's LIVE RUN 2 and LIVE RUN 3 entries are the controller-verified record of the same measurement this row restates for DECISIONS.md's own audience. |

# M5 wave A execution: task 1 (owner-approved, 2026-08-04)

Task 1 of the M5 wave A plan. Lands the `acceptance_suite` block on
`TaskSpec`, additive on `schema_version: 1`, so later tasks can author real
acceptance suites (task 5), run them as a seedcheck invariant (task 3), and
read them from the eval driver.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 137 | `AcceptanceSuiteSpec` lands in `skeptic/spec.py` above `TaskSpec` (`path: str`, `must_pass_on: list[str]`, `must_fail_on: list[str]`), and `TaskSpec` gains `acceptance_suite: AcceptanceSuiteSpec \| None = None` as a top-level field, sibling of `evaluation`, per the plan's Part 3 placement. `schema_version` stays `Literal[1]` (precedent row 98): the field is defaulted, so every task YAML on disk before this change keeps loading unchanged. A `@model_validator(mode="after")` named `_acceptance_names_resolve` on `TaskSpec` enforces the tree-name vocabulary the plan's later tasks key on: every name in `must_pass_on`/`must_fail_on` has to be `"pristine"`, `"seeded"`, or a variant id drawn from `self.evaluation.variants`, else `ValueError` names the unknown ones sorted, and `must_fail_on` has to include `"seeded"` or the suite raises: a suite that never runs against the seeded tree cannot discriminate the bug it exists to pin (plan invariant 5). The nested stub `EvaluationSpec.acceptance_tests: str \| None = None` is deleted outright, since it had zero production consumers; `extra="forbid"` meant the three test-suite files that constructed a spec carrying the key (`tests/fixtures/specs/valid-task.yaml`, `tests/helpers.py`'s minirepo YAML template, `tests/test_mutation.py`) would raise the moment the field disappeared, so the `acceptance_tests: null`/`"acceptance_tests": None` line is dropped from each in the same commit, along with `tasks/click-0001.yaml:69` and `tasks/rich-0001.yaml:112`, whose stub is simply absent until Task 5 fills the real block. | Four new tests in `tests/test_spec.py` pin the contract directly against `TaskSpec.model_validate` rather than through `load_task`'s YAML round trip, since the fixture's own values (variant ids `gold`/`gold-prime`) are exactly what the vocabulary check resolves against, and a model-level test isolates the validator from `load_task`'s own YAML-parsing and `SkepticInfraError`-wrapping concerns, which have their own coverage elsewhere in the file. `test_acceptance_suite_block_parses_and_defaults_to_none` pins both halves of the additive contract in one test: absence yields `None`, presence round-trips `path` and `must_fail_on` unchanged. `test_acceptance_suite_rejects_unknown_tree_names` pins the vocabulary check by asserting the unknown name itself, `not-a-variant`, appears in the raised message, not just that some `ValidationError` was raised. `test_acceptance_suite_requires_seeded_in_must_fail_on` pins the second half of the same validator with an empty `must_fail_on`, a distinct case from the first: a name-set check and a membership check are two different failure paths through one validator, and each needs its own red case rather than sharing one. `test_acceptance_tests_stub_is_gone` pins that the old nested key is now ordinary unknown input under `extra="forbid"`, with no special-cased backward compatibility carved out for the retired stub. | Bumping `schema_version` to 2: `acceptance_suite` is defaulted and every existing task YAML keeps loading unchanged, so the bump would buy no protection an optional field does not already provide, following row 98's own reasoning verbatim. Nesting `acceptance_suite` inside `evaluation`, where the old stub lived, rather than top-level: rejected per the plan's Part 3 placement, which the brief's interface section states as a top-level field sibling of `evaluation`, and because the suite runs against `evaluation.variants` by id, nesting it there would fold a matrix definition into the thing it validates against. Requiring `"pristine"` in `must_pass_on` (mirroring the `"seeded"` requirement on `must_fail_on`): rejected, the brief's own interface and every test fixture leave `must_pass_on` free-form beyond the closed vocabulary, and inventing a second mandatory-membership rule not in the brief would be scope creep on a schema task nine downstream tasks are blocked on. |

**Ripple:** focused suite (`tests/test_spec.py`) goes from 20 tests to 24;
full suite (`-m "not docker"`) goes from 549 to 553 passed, still 81
deselected, ruff clean. `tasks/click-0001.yaml` and `tasks/rich-0001.yaml`
still load through `load_task` with `acceptance_suite` defaulting to `None`.

# M5 wave A execution: task 2 (owner-approved, 2026-08-04)

Task 2 of the M5 wave A plan. Closes the gap rows 93(f) and 123 left open:
`check_task`'s own invariants read `SuiteResult` raw, so a quarantined flake
could still fail the very admission run quarantine exists to protect.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 138 | `check_task` gains a module-private `_drop_quarantined(result: SuiteResult, quarantine: list[str]) -> SuiteResult` in `skeptic/seedcheck.py`, applied at every invariant read that used to compare or red-set a raw `SuiteResult`: invariant 1's `first`/`second` (both stability and greenness), invariant 4's `seeded` (feeding `actual_red`), invariant 5's `gold` and `baseline` (map equality, computed per clean variant), and invariant 6's `hack` (red set, per hacked variant). `expected_red` stays `set(spec.seed.failing_tests)` unwrapped, so a task that lists a nodeid in both `failing_tests` and `quarantine` still fails `seed-red-exact` with `missing=[that id]`, a spec bug surfaced loud rather than silently absorbed. `tests/test_seedcheck.py` gains the fixture the three new tests and tasks 3-4 share: `ScriptedRunner`, a `check_task` runner whose `exec` never runs pytest, instead parsing the `--junitxml=` token out of the command, looking up an outcome map keyed by (workspace directory name, junit filename), and writing the matching xunit1 report, defaulting an unscripted key to an empty green suite; `run_check_with_outcomes`, which drives `check_task` against `helpers.make_task_spec`'s fixture spec with `clone_pinned`/`materialize`/`apply_patch` stubbed no-op on `skeptic.seedcheck`'s own bound names; and `result_named`, a one-line lookup by invariant name. | `SeedSpec.quarantine`'s own docstring (row 93(f)) promises exclusion "from every evidence rule in t1_collect and t1_outcomes", but `check_task`'s admission-time invariants were never part of that promise: they run before the evidence layer sees a candidate at all, on the seeded tree alone, reading `SuiteResult.outcomes` and `red_set()` raw. Row 123 measured the gap's live instance: `test_brokenpipeerror`'s subprocess timing race in `tasks/rich-0001.yaml`, already quarantined against `t1_outcomes`, stayed free to fail `pristine-green-x2` or `seed-red-exact` on the same non-deterministic pipe race, with nothing about rich-0001's own seed or gold patches involved. The direction matters both ways, which is what `_drop_quarantined`'s docstring states and the three new tests each pin separately: a red flake can break a green invariant (`pristine-green-x2`, `seed-red-exact`), and an outcome flip between two runs of the same flaky test can break a map-equality invariant (`gold-restores-baseline`) even when neither run is red by its own count. | Monkeypatching `skeptic.workspace.clone_pinned`/`materialize`/`apply_patch` instead of `skeptic.seedcheck`'s own names: rejected, `seedcheck.py` imports them by name (`from skeptic.workspace import ...`), so patching the `workspace` module's attribute leaves `check_task`'s already-bound reference untouched and the stub never fires. Building a fresh local git minirepo per test, mirroring `make_minirepo_task`, instead of reusing `helpers.make_task_spec`'s click-0001 fixture: rejected, the brief names `make_task_spec` directly, and `valid-task.yaml`'s pinned commit is a placeholder hash no real clone could `git archive`, so `check_task`'s repo-facing calls have to be stubbed regardless of which spec supplies them. Storing a pre-dropped `baseline` after invariant 1 and reading it raw at invariant 5, rather than wrapping both `gold` and `baseline` at the comparison site: rejected, wrapping at the read keeps every invariant's `_drop_quarantined` call visible at the line that needs it, matching the brief's "applied to every invariant comparison" framing, and dropping is idempotent so the extra call costs nothing. Excluding a nodeid that appears in both `failing_tests` and `quarantine` from `expected_red` so such a spec passes: rejected per the brief, that combination is a task-authoring bug, not a case admission should paper over. |

**Ripple:** `tests/test_seedcheck.py -k quarantin` RED before the fix
(`pristine-green-x2`, `seed-red-exact`, and `gold-restores-baseline` each
failed for the raw-comparison reason its own test names), 3 passed after.
Full `tests/test_seedcheck.py`: 20 passed in 49.87s, the slow VenvRunner
cases unchanged since none of them sets `quarantine`. Fast suite
(`-m "not docker"`) goes from 553 to 556 passed (the three new), still 81
deselected, ruff clean.
