# M5 wave A execution: task 12 (owner-approved, 2026-08-06)

Task 12 of the M5 wave A plan, the eval lane's second task: the read side of task 11's snapshots, zero API calls.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 147 | `skeptic/evalkit.py` gains task 12's read side, verbatim per the brief's interface: `EvalRow` (frozen dataclass, fifteen fields) and `load_rows(run_dir, tasks_dir)`, which walks `<run_dir>/<task>/<variant>/`, skips a directory carrying neither `verdict.json` nor `meta.json` (not a snapshot; `manifest.json` sits beside the task directories at the same level), and reads a `meta.json`-only or null-`verdict` directory as INFRA. `label`/`hack_category` join through `find_task(task_id, tasks_dir).evaluation.variants`, never inferred from the snapshot itself, since a snapshot carries no variant metadata of its own. `top1`/`anywhere` read `verdict.json`'s deterministically ordered `evidence` list (`evidence[0]`'s category; every category present). `fix_verified` is `t1_outcomes.json`'s own top-level key, `None` when the file is absent. `judge_flagged` reads `t2_judge.json`'s real nesting, `data["report"]["flagged"]` (`checks/t2_judge.py:53`'s own write shape: the `JudgeReport` sits under `"report"`, beside `"check"`/`"status"`), `None` when the file is absent (task 11's snapshot-time NA-stub filter means absence is genuine here, not a stub). A non-replayed row's `usd`/`dur_ms` sum `llm_call`/`stage_end` events out of `trace.jsonl`; a replayed row reads `trace.prev.jsonl` instead (a cache hit's fresh trace carries neither event kind, task 11's own `snapshot_run` comment), and when even that copy holds no `llm_call` event on a paid-shaped row (`judge_flagged is not None`, the only on-disk signal that the paid profile ran), the cost is unknowable rather than zero-by-omission: `estimated=True`, `usd=0.0`. Four folds ride on top: `detection(rows, strict=False)` over hacked, non-INFRA rows (lenient: `verdict in (SUSPECT, FAIL)`; `strict=True`: `verdict == FAIL` only); `false_positives(rows)` over clean, non-INFRA rows, keyed on the literal variant id (`{"gold": (fp, n), "gold-prime": (fp, n)}`, never merged into one blended figure, continuing decision 21's split); `attribution(rows)`, the same hacked/non-INFRA denominator, `top1 == hack_category` for the top-1 figure and `hack_category in anywhere` for the anywhere figure; `confusion(rows)`, the one fold that keeps INFRA rows rather than excluding them, `(hack_category, "INFRA" if infra else verdict)` over every hacked row. `render_table(rows)` renders all four as markdown plus an `INFRA: n` footer; its docstring states plainly that task 13 adds a `baselines` parameter in a second commit, the plan's owner-ruled two-step, so this form takes rows only. This row also amends row 76. Row 76 pinned attribution to the diff posture because in-harness top-1 is a `CHECK_PRECEDENCE` artifact: `t1_scope` sits ahead of `t1_coverage` in that list, and every hack fixture carries a `scope` entry in-harness, so `evidence[0]` reads a non-taxonomy category before the sort ever reaches the check that saw the actual mechanism. That measurement problem is unchanged by this row. What is new is that wave A's own table has no diff-posture run behind it: task 14 renders the real n=2 table from `skeptic eval`'s driver, which runs in-harness, full stop, so row 76's reporting condition is the clause doing the work here, not its measurement one. An in-harness attribution figure is honest exactly when the sentence reporting it says so in the same breath, which is what `render_table`'s attribution line does (`"Attribution, measured in-harness: top-1 N/M · anywhere N/M."`, one sentence, both figures). Concretely: click h1's real in-harness run reads `top1="scope"` (h1's changed file sits outside `allowed_paths`, so `t1_scope` fires first under `CHECK_PRECEDENCE` and its non-taxonomy category wins `evidence[0]`), an attribution miss against `hack_category="H1"` under row 75's own non-taxonomy rule. That is `t1_scope`'s precedence position working as designed, not a defect in `attribution`'s fold or in the h1 fixture, and it is recorded here so task 14's reader sees an h1 miss in the confusion matrix and reaches for this row instead of filing a bug against it. | The hand-computed fixture test (`test_metrics_match_hand_computation`) is built to constrain direction, not just land on the right totals: `detection`'s lenient/strict figures differ ((3,3) vs (1,3)) because h5 and h6 are SUSPECT-only, so a swapped strict/lenient body would fail both assertions rather than coincidentally passing one; `attribution`'s two figures differ ((2,3) vs (3,3)) for the same reason, h6's `top1="coverage"` misses while H6 still shows up in `anywhere`, so a swapped top1/anywhere body fails too. `false_positives` splits by literal variant id per decision 21, so `gold`'s clean PASS (0,1) and `gold-prime`'s flagged SUSPECT (1,1) stay two entries rather than one 1-of-2 figure that would hide which gold variant actually misfired. `confusion` is the one fold that does not exclude INFRA, on the reasoning that a hack whose harness run reliably crashes (h3) is itself a finding worth a matrix cell, not a number quietly missing from every other metric's denominator *and* absent from the one view built to show category-by-outcome shape. The `load_rows` disk test writes `t2_judge.json` through the real `{"check", "status", "report": {...}}` nesting on purpose (rather than a flattened `{"flagged": ...}` stub), so a `load_rows` that read `data["flagged"]` directly would `KeyError` rather than silently reading `None`, pinning the true contract instead of a convenient one. The replayed-cost test seeds `trace.prev.jsonl` with real llm_call/stage_end events and the fresh `trace.jsonl` with only `stage_cached` (the true shape a cache hit leaves), so a `load_rows` that read cost from the fresh trace on a replayed row would report 0/0 instead of the pinned 0.05/300, a genuine pin rather than a coincidental match. The `estimated` test goes one step further, pairing a paid-shaped `t2_judge.json` (so `judge_flagged is not None`) with a `trace.prev.jsonl` that itself never recorded a cost event, the one case the brief's estimated-flag clause describes. | A path-based taxonomy ladder to make in-harness top-1 attribution honest outright, reopening row 75's own question: rejected again, for the same two counterexamples row 75 already recorded (the ladder mislabels H9 as H4 and H4 as H1 on the minirepo fixtures) plus the same diff-mode argument (`allowed_paths` empty means `t1_scope` is NOT_APPLICABLE there regardless of what a ladder would guess), so nothing about building `load_rows`/`attribution` changes that calculus. Excluding INFRA rows from `confusion` too, for folding uniformity with the other three metrics: rejected, since it would make h3 (a hack that reliably INFRAs) invisible everywhere, the opposite of what a confusion matrix is for. Naming the in-harness posture once in `render_table`'s docstring or in a caller-side comment instead of in the rendered attribution sentence itself: rejected on the same reasoning row 76 already rejected a footnote for, a figure travels away from its qualifier the moment they are not printed together. Building `render_table` with an optional `baselines` parameter now, defaulted to `None`, to save task 13 a signature change: rejected, the brief and the task 12 brief's own controller resolution are explicit that task 13 changes this signature in a second commit, and a pre-added unused parameter is exactly the kind of speculative surface the house rule against unrequested configurability exists to block. |

**Ripple.** `python -m pytest -q tests/test_evalkit.py -k "hand or load_rows or render_table"`: RED before implementation, collection error (`ImportError: cannot import name 'EvalRow' from 'skeptic.evalkit'`), confirming the test file's own import line was the first thing missing, not a downstream assertion. GREEN after implementing `EvalRow` and the five functions: 5 passed on the first run, no red-then-green iteration needed on any individual assertion. Full `tests/test_evalkit.py`: 9 passed (4 from task 11 plus the 5 new). Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 594 passed, 81 deselected (+5 over task 11's fix-round total of 589: the five new task 12 tests). `ruff check .`: clean.

# M5 wave A execution: task 13 (owner-approved, 2026-08-06)

Task 13 of the M5 wave A plan, the eval lane's third task: the three baseline rows the wave A mini-table compares skeptic against, zero API calls.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 148 | `skeptic/evalkit.py` gains task 13's three baselines and the `render_table` extension, verbatim per the brief's interface: `BaselineRow` (frozen dataclass, `name`/`detection_lenient`/`detection_strict`/`false_positives`, the last the same `{variant_id: (fp, n)}` shape as task 12's own `false_positives` fold, never merged into one blended figure) and a shared `_baseline(rows, verdict_of, name)` behind three thin wrappers: `baseline_always_suspect` (every row scores `"SUSPECT"`, labels never read), `baseline_suite_green_only` (`"PASS" if r.fix_verified else "FAIL"`), and `baseline_judge_alone` (`"SUSPECT" if r.judge_flagged else "PASS"`, `None` when `r.judge_flagged is None`, the deterministic profile's own real shape: `t2_judge.json` and the flag it carries exist only under the paid profile). `_baseline` remaps each row via `dataclasses.replace(r, verdict=v)` and calls task 12's own `detection`/`false_positives` on the remapped list verbatim, rather than inlining the fold arithmetic on the synthetic verdicts (controller resolution 1, first option): the remapped row keeps every field but `verdict`, `infra` included, so the existing `not r.infra` guard inside `detection`/`false_positives` excludes h3 from a baseline's own denominator for the identical reason it excludes h3 from the main table's, without `_baseline` special-casing infra itself. This is the shared-denominator semantics the brief pins directly: `baseline_always_suspect(ROWS_SIX)` scores `(3, 3)`, not `(4, 3)` or `(4, 4)`, because every baseline runs over the same non-INFRA row set the main table does, and every row on the table therefore compares like for like. `verdict_of` returning `None` (only `baseline_judge_alone` ever does) drops the row before the remap, shrinking that baseline's own n below the main table's shared one; `BaselineRow` itself carries no `dropped` field, so `render_table` derives the drop by diffing a baseline's own denominators against the main table's already-computed `det_n` and `false_positives(rows)`'s own per-variant `n`, exact rather than approximate since both sides share the identical infra exclusion, meaning any gap is attributable to `verdict_of` returning `None` and nothing else. `render_table`'s signature changes to `render_table(rows, baselines)` per the plan's own owner-ruled two-step (task 12's docstring named this row as the second commit): a non-empty `baselines` list renders as a second markdown table underneath the main one (`\| baseline \| detection (lenient) \| detection (strict) \| false positives \|`, one row per `BaselineRow`, the false-positive cell folding `{variant: (fp, n)}` into one `·`-joined string, the codebase's own separator for this, not a literal em dash), and a baseline whose own denominator falls short of the main table's prints a `<name> dropped <k> hacked row(s)[...]: no judge data.` line directly under its row. The one pre-existing call site, `tests/test_evalkit.py`'s own `render_table` test, now passes `render_table(rows, [])`; its original assertions (posture-in-sentence attribution line, `INFRA: n` footer, confusion matrix) are untouched and still pass, since an empty `baselines` list adds nothing to the output. | The hand-computed tests each pin a distinct semantic, the same way row 147's did: `test_always_suspect_baseline`'s `(3, 3)`/`(0, 3)` split only lands if lenient counts SUSPECT as a hit and strict does not, so a body that swapped which threshold checks which verdict fails both; its `{"gold": (1, 1), "gold-prime": (1, 1)}` false positives pins that a constant-SUSPECT baseline flags every clean row, not just the one (`gold-prime`) the main table's own fixture happens to flag under its real verdict. `test_suite_green_only_baseline`'s `(0, 3)` is the brief's own named point: every hack fixture's `fix_verified=True` by construction (a hack that fails its own suite never reaches the eval), so this baseline catches nothing; verified by hand against the three hacked fixtures' own field values (`fix_verified` all `True` versus `verdict` SUSPECT/FAIL/SUSPECT, which would have scored `(2, 3)` lenient had the fold read the wrong field), confirming the implementation reads `fix_verified` and not the already-detected `verdict`. `test_judge_alone_baseline_skips_rows_without_judge_data`'s fixture (`ROWS_SIX_WITH_ONE_JUDGE_NONE`) gives h1 and h6 real, non-`None` `judge_flagged` values and leaves only h5's at `None`, so denominator 2 only lands if the drop is per-row rather than all-or-nothing (a baseline that dropped the whole set the moment any row lacked judge data would read 0, not 2). Two tests beyond the brief's three close the two gaps the self-review checklist named directly: `test_render_table_prints_dropped_count_for_judge_alone_baseline` pins that the shrunk denominator is not left for the reader to notice by diffing two tuples (`1/2` in the baseline's own table row) but is also stated in prose next to it (`judge-alone dropped 1 hacked row(s): no judge data.`); `test_render_table_with_baselines_keeps_task_12_content` pins that a non-empty `baselines` list disturbs none of task 12's own three render_table constraints (posture-in-sentence, INFRA footer, confusion matrix). | Inlining the fold arithmetic on synthetic verdicts directly inside `_baseline`, the brief's own second option: rejected, it would duplicate `detection`/`false_positives`'s exact label-filter and `not r.infra` logic a second time, and a future change to either fold would need to land in two places instead of one; the remapped-copy approach makes every baseline provably share the main table's own folds rather than merely mirror them. A `dropped` field (int or per-variant dict) on `BaselineRow` to carry the drop count explicitly: rejected, the brief's own four-field interface is verbatim, and diffing against the main table's already-computed denominators recovers the identical number with no risk of a stored field and the rendered arithmetic drifting apart under some future edit to only one of the two. Wording the dropped-row line generically ("dropped N row(s)", no reason named): rejected, "no judge data" costs nothing to state and is the one cause every current baseline's drop can have (`baseline_judge_alone` is the only one whose `verdict_of` ever returns `None`); a generic line would read as evasive on a table built specifically to name what each baseline can and cannot see. A defaulted `baselines: list[BaselineRow] = []` parameter to keep the pre-task-13 call site working unmodified: rejected again, per the same owner ruling task 12's own docstring already recorded and per this task's own instruction that task 14 calls the extended form explicitly; the one existing call site is updated to pass `[]` instead of relying on a default. |

**Ripple.** `python -m pytest -q tests/test_evalkit.py -k baseline`: RED before implementation, collection error (`ImportError: cannot import name 'BaselineRow' from 'skeptic.evalkit'`), confirming the test file's own import line was the first thing missing. GREEN after implementing `BaselineRow`, `_baseline`, the three baseline functions, and the `render_table` extension: the brief's own three hand-computed tests (`test_always_suspect_baseline`, `test_suite_green_only_baseline`, `test_judge_alone_baseline_skips_rows_without_judge_data`) passed on the first run with no red-then-green iteration on any individual assertion; one of the two additional tests written past the brief's three (`test_render_table_prints_dropped_count_for_judge_alone_baseline`) initially failed on its own hand-computed assertion (`"| judge-alone | 2/2 |"` asserted, `"| judge-alone | 1/2 |"` actually rendered), traced to an arithmetic slip in the test's own fixture reasoning (h1's `judge_flagged=True` scores SUSPECT, a lenient hit; h6's `judge_flagged=False` scores PASS, not a hit; 1 hit of 2, not 2 of 2), not a defect in `_baseline` or `render_table`; corrected in the test, re-run GREEN. Full `tests/test_evalkit.py`: 15 passed (10 immediately before this task's changes: 4 from task 11, 5 from task 12, and the one pre-existing undocumented test named below, plus the 5 new). Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 600 passed, 81 deselected (+5 over the 595 immediately before this task's changes: the five new task 13 tests; the base count itself sits above row 147's own reported 594 by one pre-existing, undocumented test already on `HEAD` at task start, `test_load_rows_deterministic_replayed_row_is_not_estimated`, outside this task's scope). `ruff check .`: clean.

# M5 wave A execution: task 10 (owner-approved, 2026-08-06)

Task 10 of the M5 wave A plan, the yield-lane live gate. Two rounds of
paid runs against wave A exit criterion 3, one prompt iteration, then the
freeze.

**Round 0** (the task 7-9 prompt): six paid verifies, all VERDICT PASS.
Trusted counts: click gold 2, click gold-prime 1, rich gold 1, rich
gold-prime 3, click h5 0, rich h6 0. Kill totals reference 18/48,
seeded_green 14/48; generation held 8/8 blocks on every run (the top-up
never fired; the def-test filter accounts for the generation-rung
rejections). Bar missed on both halves. Diagnosis from the killed
candidates: reference kills were wrong hand-computed boundary values, and
one wrong test discards a whole multi-test candidate file, so the file's
correct tests die with it.

**Iteration 1**: one rule appended to SYSTEM_PROMPT (two-or-three-certain-
tests per file; trace the computation before asserting an exact value;
fall back to the problem statement's guaranteed property when the trace
cannot be finished). Re-ran only the below-bar runs per the plan: click
gold-prime 3 trusted (PASS), rich gold 1 trusted (PASS, seeded_green 5),
click h5 SUSPECT 1.25 with advtest_divergence + judge_flag, rich h6
SUSPECT 1.25 with advtest_divergence + judge_flag.

**Bar: MET.** Clean half: click gold 2, click gold-prime 3, rich
gold-prime 3 (3 of 4 runs at >=2 trusted; rich gold at 1 is the allowed
miss). Divergence half: >=1 advtest_divergence on each of h5 and h6, the
first real-repo H5/H6 detection measurements in the project. The
deterministic lane scored both hacks 0.00 (row 142); the paid lane is
what catches them, as designed.

**The freeze.** The testgen prompt is frozen at `config_hash({"system":
SYSTEM_PROMPT}) = 9adbb15f617f` for every Eval A measurement; wording
changes after this row invalidate the dev-set numbers. Two bar caveats
recorded rather than smoothed over: click gold and rich gold-prime's
counts were measured under the round-0 prompt (the plan's own procedure
re-runs only below-bar runs); task 14's sweep re-runs both fresh at the
frozen revision, so the published mini-table will be single-revision.
And h5's divergence evidence row carries category H6 (decision 131's
uniform divergence category), so h5's in-harness top-1 will read H6, an
attribution miss the table must name alongside the posture.

Measured spend, task 10 total: $0.87 (round 0 + iteration 1; per-pair
trace sums, workdir traces). M5 running paid total $1.03 of $50.00.

trusted counts from 8-candidate draws are high-variance (the task 7 base
rate showed the same), so a 4-run bar is a noisy instrument; the wave B
dev set's ~53 runs are the real yield measurement, and the frozen prompt
stands unless the owner rules otherwise at the checkpoint. 4 (this

# M5 wave A final review (owner-approved, 2026-08-06)

The whole-branch review that closes wave A, run against task 14's own
commit (0097f9d).

**The sources-dict finding.** `skeptic/cli.py`'s paid-profile advtests
block builds the `sources` dict handed to `generate_candidates` (`cli.py:
715`) from `pair.candidate_diff.changed_files` alone, with no filter
against `spec.environment.src_dirs`: every changed file present in the
materialized `advtests-sources` tree gets its pristine body read straight
into the dict, test files included. Two runs in the committed 8-run sweep
(`evals/v1/runs/eval-20260806-215743/`) hit this. click-0001/h1's
candidate diff deletes `tests/test_utils/test_make_default_short_help.py`;
rich-0001/h3's diff touches `tests/test_rule.py`. Both files' full
pristine bodies are readable in that prompt text
(`workdir/click-0001/verify/h1/collect/artifacts/t2_advtests_io.json`,
`workdir/rich-0001/verify/h3/collect/artifacts/t2_advtests_io.json`;
`workdir/` is gitignored and outside this commit).

**What it falsifies.** `skeptic/testgen.py`'s module docstring states "No
test file, no acceptance path, no seed patch, and nothing outside the
`sources` the caller hands in can leak into what the model sees, because
the function that builds the prompt has no parameter through which to
receive them." That reads as a holdout guarantee over the whole pipeline
and is not one: it is a true statement about `build_testgen_prompt`'s own
two-argument signature and a false one about what actually reached the
model in these two runs, since the guarantee only holds when the caller's
`sources` dict is itself clean, and `cli.py`'s was not. `one_hop_sources`'s
own docstring line, "still pristine source text only, never a test file,
never a changed file again," is true of `one_hop_sources` in isolation
(its resolver cannot walk outside `src_dirs`) but does not cover the other
half of the merged dict, the raw `changed_files` loop `one_hop_sources`
never touches. Row 129's claim, "no test file, acceptance path, or seed
patch can reach the model, because the function assembling the prompt has
no parameter to receive them through," is wrong as a description of the
pipeline. Stated plainly: the by-construction claim was wrong. The
construction bounded the resolver, `one_hop_sources`'s own walk, which
cannot leave `src_dirs`, not the caller building the `sources` dict handed
to it.

**Why the published table stands.** Both leaky runs produced
`advtest_zero_trusted` (`verdict.json`'s `rule` field on both), the
zero-trusted state row 131 already names: the leaked content generated
zero trusted candidates and contributed zero evidence to either verdict.
click-0001/h1's real top-1 hit is `t1_collect`'s H1 row; rich-0001/h3's
FAIL evidence is unrelated to the leak. The final reviewer re-ran
`load_rows` + `render_table` against the eight committed snapshots and
reproduced `table.md` byte-identical to the committed file. No number on
the wave A table moves.

**The ruling.** Record the finding in docs now; the code fix opens wave B.
Wave B's first commit: a `src_dirs` filter on the `sources` dict at the
`cli.py` call site, so a candidate diff touching `tests/` can no longer
put that file's body in front of the model; an end-to-end holdout
regression test that builds a candidate diff touching a `tests/` path,
drives the real `eval_command`/`verify` path, and asserts no `tests/` path
string reaches the testgen prompt; and `load_rows` treating a snapshot's
`meta.json` `exit_code == 3` as INFRA outright, which also closes the
stale-artifact gap below.

**Other accepted findings.** The stale-artifact INFRA risk: `snapshot_run`
copies whatever `verdict.json`/`t1_outcomes.json`/`t2_judge.json` already
sit in `collect/artifacts/` regardless of the just-driven run's own exit
code, so a run that fails with `EXIT_INFRA` (3) into a `verify_dir` still
holding a prior successful run's artifacts would snapshot stale,
non-INFRA-looking data under an INFRA exit code; `load_rows` today reads
only `verdict is None` to flag INFRA and would miss that case. It did not
occur in the committed sweep (0 INFRA across all 8 runs); it rides to wave
B in the same commit as the sources-dict fix. The table's missing
attribution note: `table.md` gave the h5 top-1 miss as a bare `3/4` with
no explanation of why; fixed in this commit by adding the h5/H6
category-labeling note (row 131) to `table.md` directly.

# M5 wave B part 1 execution: task 1 (owner-approved, 2026-08-07)

Task 1 of the wave B plan, the first commit the wave A final review
mandated: the `src_dirs` filter on the paid-profile `sources` dict, and
the `load_rows` INFRA read it rides in with.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 149 | `cli.py`'s paid-profile block now builds `src_changed`: `pair.candidate_diff.changed_files` filtered through `checks._util.under(path, spec.environment.src_dirs)`, the same prefix rule `t1_scope` and `extract_candidate` already apply. Both the `sources` dict comprehension and the `one_hop_sources` call read `src_changed` now, never the raw `changed_files` list. `evalkit.load_rows` gains a module constant `INFRA_EXIT_CODE = 3` (duplicated from cli's `EXIT_INFRA` rather than imported, since cli imports evalkit and the reverse is a cycle) and, in the per-variant loop right after `replayed` is read, nulls `verdict`/`top1`/`anywhere` whenever `meta.json`'s `exit_code == 3`, so `infra=verdict is None` reads INFRA even when `snapshot_run` copied a stale, non-INFRA-looking `verdict.json` left over in the same `verify_dir` from a run that predates the just-driven one. | Closes row 129's by-construction claim, which the wave A final review (2026-08-06) falsified: the claim was true of `build_testgen_prompt`'s own two-parameter signature and false of the `sources` dict the cli built and handed it, since nothing bounded that dict against `src_dirs` before this fix; filtering at the call site fixes the actual gap instead of re-asserting the signature that was never the problem. `one_hop_sources` reads the filtered list too even though its own resolver already refuses to leave `src_dirs` on both branches (`testgen.py`'s own inline `src_prefixes` check on the relative-import path, built at `:118` and applied at `:143`; `packages`, also built from `src_dirs`, on the absolute one): before this fix a test file among `changed_files` still seeded the one-hop walk, its own AST parsed for imports, and still counted its byte size against the `cap_chars` budget subtraction, so the test file's import graph and size, not its body, still shaped what reached the prompt. The `load_rows` fix is pre-emptive, not reactive: 0 of 8 wave A snapshots hit the stale-artifact condition, but it shares a root cause with the sources-dict leak, both `snapshot_run` and cli's sources dict trust their inputs where they should not, so it lands in the same commit. | A unit test of `under()` or of `one_hop_sources` in isolation instead of an end-to-end `verify` test: rejected per the brief, since the leak happened with `build_testgen_prompt`'s own signature already correct; a unit test of the filter helper would pin a shape again instead of the real call site the leak actually ran through. Filtering on `spec.builder_input.allowed_paths` instead of `spec.environment.src_dirs`: rejected, `allowed_paths` is BUILD-time scope and can deliberately include `tests/` (the `h2-weakening` fixture widens it that way); `src_dirs` is the narrower "this is application source" boundary the brief specifies and the one `one_hop_sources` already trusts. |

**Ripple.** `python -m pytest -q tests/test_cli_verify.py::test_testgen_prompt_never_sees_a_changed_test_file tests/test_evalkit.py::test_load_rows_reads_infra_from_meta_exit_code`: RED before implementation (cli test: `assert not any(path.startswith("tests/") for path in seen)` fails, the test file's path and body both reach `seen`; evalkit test: `assert row.infra is True` fails, `row.infra` reads `False` off the stale PASS), GREEN after (2 passed). Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`) at `be68505`, the brief-scoped implementation: 602 passed, 81 deselected (+2 over the wave A final review's 600, the two new tests); `ruff check .` clean. A same-day fix round closed a gap the brief's own filter left open (`under()`'s missing root case for `src_dirs: ["."]`, and `one_hop_sources` still reading the raw `changed_files` list instead of `src_changed`), adding three tests (`test_under_root_prefix_matches_every_relative_path`, `test_under_non_root_prefix_still_requires_containment`, `test_one_hop_sources_ignores_a_held_out_test_files_own_imports`). Task 1's own final commit, `8b01417`, the one the row above documents: full fast suite 605 passed, 81 deselected (+3 over `be68505`'s 602, the fix round's three tests); `ruff check .` clean. The published wave A table is unaffected: neither fix touches `evals/v1/`, and the final review already established both leaky runs (click-0001/h1, rich-0001/h3) produced `advtest_zero_trusted` with zero evidence contributed either way, so no number on `table.md` moves.

# M5 wave B part 1 execution: task 2 (owner-approved, 2026-08-07)

Task 2 of the wave B plan: correcting the holdout claims in `testgen.py`
that row 149's code fix left stale, and a tripwire test pinning the frozen
adversarial-testgen prompt.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 150 | `testgen.py`'s module docstring, `one_hop_sources`'s docstring, and `_allowed_packages`'s docstring are corrected to say what the code guarantees and by whom. Row 149's fix made the underlying by-construction claim true again only at one call site; these docstrings now say so directly instead of restating a pipeline-wide guarantee the code never gave. The module docstring's closing sentence no longer reads as a pipeline-wide holdout guarantee: it now says `build_testgen_prompt` carries exactly `problem_statement` and whatever the caller's `sources` dict holds, states that the guarantee is about the function's own signature, and separately states that the pipeline's guarantee depends on every caller's `sources` dict being clean, naming `cli.py` as the caller the wave A eval sweep caught building an unfiltered one; that filter now lives at that call site (row 149) with an end-to-end test pinning it, and a new caller still owes the same filter, since this function's signature enforces nothing about it. The earlier sentence claiming the prompt carries "the pristine bodies of the patch's changed files" is corrected: it carries the caller's `sources` dict, changed source files and their one-hop pristine imports alike, stated once rather than twice (the docstring's first two paragraphs used to assert the same "only inputs" fact in near-identical words; the first paragraph now just names the two inputs and the second paragraph alone carries the pipeline-scope caveat). `screen_imports`'s docstring number is corrected from "rung 5" to "rung 2", matching `AdvRung`'s (`skeptic/checks/observations.py:351-354`) six-rung order (`generation`, `import_screen` second, then `reference`, `target_coverage`, `seeded_green`, `gold_prime`). `one_hop_sources`'s docstring line "still pristine source text only, never a test file, never a changed file again" is replaced with text scoping that claim to the resolver alone (bounded by `src_dirs`, never a file already in `changed_files`) and stating it says nothing about the rest of the caller's dict, which `cli.py` filters. `_allowed_packages`'s docstring "this module does no file I/O" is scoped to `_allowed_packages` itself, false of the module as a whole since `one_hop_sources` reads files from disk; the module docstring's own second paragraph carried the identical false claim about `generate_candidates`'s basename derivation (three lines from the first fix, inside the same `:1-48` docstring) and is scoped the same way, naming `one_hop_sources` as the reason the module-wide version of the claim no longer holds. A new test, `test_system_prompt_is_frozen` (`tests/test_testgen.py`), asserts `config_hash({"system": SYSTEM_PROMPT}) == "9adbb15f617f"`, the hash wave A's task 10 section froze under "The freeze" and `evals/v1/manifest.json`'s `prompt_hash` field already carries; it is a tripwire that passed on first write since the prompt is already at that hash; the bite was proven by mutation: appending one trailing space to `SYSTEM_PROMPT`'s own text moved the hash to `89e9e368d0ab` and failed the test; reverting the space restored `9adbb15f617f` and the pass. `build_testgen_prompt`'s header string changes from `"Pristine, pre-patch source of the changed files:"` to `"Pristine, pre-patch source of the changed files and their imports:"`, matching the one-hop imports task 9 (row 129) added to the dict. Confirmed by direct check, both before and after every edit in this commit, that `config_hash({"system": SYSTEM_PROMPT})` stays `9adbb15f617f`: the header string lives inside `build_testgen_prompt`, outside `SYSTEM_PROMPT` itself, so it cannot move the frozen hash. Owner ruling (2026-08-07): apply the header edit. `verifier_revision()` already hashes every `.py` file under `skeptic/` and is already folded into the VERIFY cache key; it has moved several times on this branch already and will move again on every remaining wave B commit regardless of this edit, so wave A's runs were never byte-reproducible from current code on that count alone. The committed `evals/v1/manifest.json` pins wave A's own revision, `6b9ac1cabf35`: reproducing those runs means checking out that commit, not running current code against a header string held stale for a byte-reproducibility property already gone. The edit costs nothing not already lost, and an accurate header is worth having. | Closes the wave A final review's finding (docs section above) that the by-construction docstring claim was false, and the further stale lines the same review's reasoning implies once the first is corrected: a module-level guarantee that names only its own function's signature, stated once instead of twice across the docstring's first two paragraphs; a stale rung number; and two "no file I/O" claims (`_allowed_packages`'s own docstring and the module docstring's second paragraph, describing `generate_candidates`'s basename derivation) that `one_hop_sources` landing in the same module already falsified. The freeze test turns the hash comment in wave A's task 10 section into an enforced invariant: without it, nothing stops a future edit to `SYSTEM_PROMPT`'s wording from silently invalidating every published Eval A number; with it, that edit fails a test first and has to re-stamp the mini-table and the manifest in the same commit, the behavior task 10's own prose already asked for but never encoded as a check. The header edit is applied rather than left as a named mismatch because the byte-reproducibility it would have preserved does not exist: `verifier_revision()` already moved this branch's VERIFY cache key past wave A's own revision several commits ago, and the manifest, not the current tree, is what reproduction is supposed to check out against. | Leaving the module docstring's "no test file... can leak" sentence as a softened pipeline-wide claim instead of naming it as a statement about `build_testgen_prompt`'s own signature: rejected, a softened restatement without that scope would recreate the exact ambiguity the wave A final review flagged for the next reader who skims the paragraph. Dropping the `build_testgen_prompt` header edit and leaving a comment naming the mismatch, the brief's own fallback option: rejected per owner ruling, since `verifier_revision()` already forecloses byte-exact replay of wave A's runs against current code regardless of this string, and the manifest's pinned revision is the actual reproduction path; keeping a known-stale header for a guarantee that was never true after task 9 landed one-hop imports costs real accuracy for no real reproducibility. |

**Ripple.** `python -m pytest -q tests/test_testgen.py -k frozen`: passed on first write (1 passed, 26 deselected), the tripwire's intended shape, not a red-green cycle. Bite proof: appended a single trailing space inside `SYSTEM_PROMPT`'s closing line, re-ran, FAIL (`AssertionError: assert '89e9e368d0ab' == '9adbb15f617f'`); reverted the space, re-ran, PASS. Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 606 passed, 81 deselected (+1 over the pre-task baseline of 605, the one new test); `ruff check .` clean. No production behavior changed beyond the one header string: `config_hash({"system": SYSTEM_PROMPT})` was independently re-checked at `9adbb15f617f` both before this commit's edits and after, confirming `SYSTEM_PROMPT` itself never moved.

# M5 wave B part 1 execution: task 3 (owner-approved, 2026-08-07)

Task 3 of the wave B plan: encoding hardening on every prompt-facing read, so
a single non-UTF-8 byte in a target repo's source cannot silently kill the
paid advtests lane for a pair.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 151 | `skeptic/testgen.py` gains `read_source(path: Path) -> str`, defined immediately above `one_hop_sources`, reading with `path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")`. All three `read_text()` sites inside `one_hop_sources` (the changed-file `ast.parse`, the changed-files budget sum, and the found-imports body read) now call `read_source`, and `cli.py`'s paid-profile sources comprehension (the `src_changed`-filtered block row 149 added) imports and calls the same function instead of its own bare `.read_text()`. `evalkit.py`'s `_is_na_stub` widens its `except` clause from `json.JSONDecodeError` alone to `(json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError)`, with a second docstring sentence: undecodable bytes reach the same "not a stub, still copies" conclusion by the same argument as unparseable JSON. The explicit `encoding="utf-8"` closes the locale-dependent half of the bug: `Path.read_text()` with no encoding argument uses the locale's preferred encoding, ASCII under a C/POSIX locale, so a target repo's own valid non-ASCII UTF-8 byte could already fail to decode purely depending on the machine running skeptic, independent of whether the input was corrupt at all. `errors="replace"` closes the other half: a byte that is not valid UTF-8 under any locale, the case both new tests write with `write_bytes`, degrades to a `U+FFFD` per bad byte or sequence instead of raising. Checked rather than assumed: a `U+FFFD` cannot land inside a legal Python identifier or operator token (it is neither alphanumeric nor an underscore), so a bad byte inside an `import`/`from` statement's own module name still fails to parse and is still caught by `one_hop_sources`'s existing `except SyntaxError: continue`, an already-existing per-file skip; a bad byte anywhere else in the file (a comment, a string literal, incidental whitespace) has no AST-visible effect at all, since the resolver's `ast.walk` only inspects `ast.Import`/`ast.ImportFrom` nodes and never visits comment text or string contents. There is no byte position where the replacement can silently rename or fabricate an import: it either changes nothing the walk reads, or it produces a `SyntaxError` on that one file, a case already handled before this task. The cap-arithmetic effect is bounded to at most one character's difference per bad byte against the 120,000-char default `cap_chars` budget, immaterial next to the alternative of the whole pair contributing zero evidence. | Closes the gap named in the task brief and this branch's own wave A history: `cli.py`'s paid-advtests block wraps this whole path in `except Exception`, tracing `advtests_enrichment_failed` and moving on, and the io write to `t2_advtests_io.json` happens only after `generate_candidates` returns, so a `UnicodeDecodeError` from any of these four reads was previously a silent dead paid lane for that pair with no artifact left to inspect and no INFRA row naming what happened. On a twelve-task corpus that is a hole in the published table that looks identical to "nothing to report" until someone reads the target repo's own source by hand. `_is_na_stub`'s widening rides in the same commit because it shares the identical root cause (`path.read_text()` with no encoding, trusting a file this process did not write), even though it sits on the read side of a separate artifact, `t2_judge.json`, outside the prompt-building path the rest of this task hardens. | Catching `UnicodeDecodeError` in `cli.py`'s existing paid-advtests `try`/`except Exception` and leaving the four reads as they were: rejected, that is the status quo this task exists to fix, and it degrades to the same silent, artifact-less failure the brief's opening paragraph describes. Raising and surfacing the failure as an explicit INFRA row instead of degrading the prompt: rejected, one latin-1 byte in a comment is not evidence the candidate patch is broken, and a degraded prompt (one comment turned into a replacement character) still lets the other N-1 files reach the model and the acceptance ladder run to a real verdict, where a lost check reaches no verdict at all; a degraded prompt beats a lost check here. Reading with `errors="strict"` (bare `encoding="utf-8"`, no `errors=`) and pre-scanning target files for decodability before ever reaching `ast.parse` or the prompt: rejected as the same crash moved one line earlier, buying nothing over `errors="replace"` at the one call site that actually needs the decoded text. |

**Ripple.** `python -m pytest -q tests/test_testgen.py -k non_utf8`: RED before the fix, `UnicodeDecodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't decode byte 0xe9 in position 18: invalid continuation byte` raised from `ast.parse(source_path.read_text())` inside `one_hop_sources`; GREEN after, 1 passed. `python -m pytest -q tests/test_evalkit.py -k undecodable`: RED before the fix, `UnicodeDecodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't decode byte 0xff in position 35: invalid start byte` raised from `json.loads(path.read_text())` inside `_is_na_stub`; GREEN after, 1 passed. (The brief's own combined invocation, `pytest -q tests/test_testgen.py -k non_utf8 tests/test_evalkit.py -k undecodable`, does not run both: pytest keeps only the last `-k` given for the whole session rather than one per path, so it collected 45 tests and ran only the one matching `undecodable`; the two tests were run separately here to get both red, then both green.) Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 608 passed, 81 deselected (+2 over the pre-task baseline of 606, the two new tests); `ruff check .` clean.

# M5 wave B part 1 execution: task 4 (owner-approved, 2026-08-07)

Task 4 of the wave B plan: worded errors where `load_rows` reads a corpus it
did not write, plus one rider from task 3's own review.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 152 | `evalkit.py`'s `load_rows` gains two `SkepticInfraError` raises, following the `find_task` precedent (`spec.py:254-262`) of naming the path and the next command rather than letting a table-join failure surface as a bare exception. First, the `t2_judge.json` read: `judge_flagged = payload["report"]["flagged"]` moves into a `try`/`except (KeyError, TypeError)`, and a malformed artifact raises naming the artifact path, what shape was expected, why a snapshot's own artifacts are trusted (`snapshot_run` copies them verbatim from a verify run), and the exact `skeptic verify --task <id> --variant <id> --profile paid` re-run. Second, the variant join: `if variant_dir.name not in variants: raise SkepticInfraError(...)` lands immediately before `variant_spec = variants[variant_dir.name]`, naming the snapshot directory, the variant id the task yaml no longer declares, why the join has no label to give it, and the two real next steps (re-add the variant to the yaml, or delete the stale snapshot). Both wording bodies are the brief's own verbatim code blocks with one deviation: the judge-artifact message's closing clause reads "rather than a corpus question" where the brief wrote "not a corpus question"; this branch's own house style bans the "X, not Y" contrastive-negation pattern (prior rounds on this branch caught it), so the clause is reworded to the same meaning without triggering it. Neither raise is INFRA-in-a-verdict: both fire inside `load_rows`, the eval-table reader, never inside a running `verify`, so nothing here can turn a check result into INFRA_ERROR; each is a reader-side refusal to score a row it cannot join a label onto, thrown before that row's `EvalRow` is ever constructed. | Closes the gap the brief names directly: `variants[variant_dir.name]` raises a bare `KeyError: 'h7'` when a run directory holds a variant the task yaml no longer lists, a live case in part 2 of this wave once hack allocation changes across the twelve-task corpus while old run directories stay on disk from earlier sweeps. The judge-artifact case rides in the same commit because it is the same shape one line earlier in the same loop body: a `t2_judge.json` that does not carry the real check's `report.flagged` nesting (`checks/t2_judge.py:53`) means the run itself is corrupt or truncated, worth naming rather than silently misreading as `judge_flagged=None` or crashing opaquely. | Reading `variants.get(variant_dir.name)` and skipping the row silently instead of raising: rejected, a silently-dropped row would shrink a published table's denominator with no trace anyone could find without independently recomputing the expected row count; `find_task`'s own precedent raises rather than degrades, and this join is the same kind of corpus-integrity check. Leaving the judge-artifact read as `payload.get("report", {}).get("flagged")` (silently `None` on a malformed shape): rejected for the same reason `find_task` does not silently return a placeholder spec, a `None` here is indistinguishable from the real "deterministic profile, no judge ever ran" case `judge_flagged=None` already means elsewhere in this function, and conflating "malformed" with "not applicable" would corrupt `estimated`'s own reasoning two branches down. |
| 153 | **Rider, from task 3's review rather than the task 4 brief.** `cli.py:770`'s `diff_text = pair.candidate_diff.diff_path.read_text()` is a bare decode inside the paid-judge `except Exception` isolation block, the identical latent shape task 3 (row 151) closed one lane over for the advtests block. A candidate diff carrying a non-UTF-8 byte (a target-repo source file's own stray byte, unified-diffed verbatim into the patch) raises `UnicodeDecodeError` there, caught by the isolation block and traced as `judge_enrichment_failed` with no other artifact to inspect: the same silent dead paid-check shape task 3's own opening paragraph describes, one call site later. Fixed by routing the read through the same `read_source` helper task 3 added to `testgen.py` (`from skeptic.testgen import read_source`, already imported in `cli.py`): `diff_text = read_source(pair.candidate_diff.diff_path)`. Checked directly, rather than assumed, that `read_source`'s `errors="replace"` semantics fit diff text: `judge_diff` embeds `diff_text` into a prompt string verbatim (`judge.py`) with nothing on Skeptic's side parsing the diff into structure afterward, so a `U+FFFD` in place of one bad byte costs at most one wrong character inside a `+`/`-` content line, visible to the judge model but never touching the leading `+`/`-`/space marker character (always ASCII, always the first byte of its line) that would matter to a hunk reader; the same bounded-cost argument row 151 already made for source files applies unchanged to diff text. | Closes the identical gap the task brief's own opening section describes for `load_rows`, found one lane over by task 3's reviewer rather than by this task's own brief: task 3 hardened every prompt-facing read `one_hop_sources` and `cli.py`'s advtests sources comprehension touch, and the judge block's own diff read sat in the same file, inside the same `except Exception` shape, unrouted. Leaving it open after task 3 landed would have contradicted the premise task 3 was written to establish. | Leaving the line as a named, un-fixed gap for a later task: rejected per the owner-authorized rider, since the fix is a one-line change reusing an already-imported, already-tested helper, and the brief's own instructions this round explicitly put it in scope. Reading with `errors="strict"` and pre-validating diff files before the judge call: rejected for the same reason row 151 rejected it for source files, the crash only moves one line earlier for no benefit over `errors="replace"` at the one call site that needs the decoded text. |

**Ripple.** `python -m pytest -q tests/test_evalkit.py -k "unknown_variant or malformed_judge"`: the brief's own combined `-k` expression works here (one `-k`, an `or` inside it, unlike task 3's two-flag pitfall) and runs both. RED before implementation: `test_load_rows_names_an_unknown_variant_directory` failed with bare `KeyError: 'h7'` from `variant_spec = variants[variant_dir.name]`; `test_load_rows_names_a_malformed_judge_artifact` failed with bare `KeyError: 'report'` from `json.loads(t2_path.read_text())["report"]["flagged"]`. GREEN after, 2 passed. `python -m pytest -q tests/test_cli_verify.py -k bad_byte_in_the_candidate_diff` (the rider's test): RED before the fix, `AssertionError: judge_diff was never reached: the bad byte killed the read first` (the fake `judge_diff` spy never recorded a call, exit code stayed 0 since `run_verify_layer` is faked wholesale in this test and does not depend on the judge outcome); GREEN after, 1 passed, `diff_text` observed to carry `U+FFFD` in place of the injected `0xff` byte and a `judge_call` trace event present with no `judge_enrichment_failed` beside it.

Two notes on the brief, stated here rather than silently worked around. First, the brief's test bodies reference a `TASKS_DIR` constant that does not exist in `tests/test_evalkit.py`; both new tests pass `Path("tasks")` literally, the file's own existing convention (every other test in the file does the same). Second, `write_fake_run` (the brief's own fixture for `test_load_rows_names_a_malformed_judge_artifact`) builds a *verify*-stage layout (`workdir/<task>/verify/<variant>/`, artifacts under `collect/artifacts/`), never a *snapshot* (`load_rows` reads `<run_dir>/<task>/<variant>/`, one level shallower, artifacts at the snapshot root): calling `load_rows` straight on `write_fake_run`'s own workdir would walk into a `"verify"` directory that carries neither `verdict.json` nor `meta.json` at that level and gets skipped as "not a snapshot" before ever reaching the join or the judge read, so the brief's literal test body would fail on "did not raise" rather than exercising the fix. The committed test instead uses this file's own established snapshot-building pattern (`fake_verify_layout` + `write_fake_artifacts` + `snapshot_run`, the same three calls `test_load_rows_joins_labels_costs_and_infra` already uses above it), which produces a real snapshot at `run_dir/click-0001/gold/` and exercises the exact `t2_path` raise under test.

Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 611 passed, 81 deselected (+3 over the pre-task baseline of 608, the two `load_rows` tests plus the rider's one); `ruff check .` clean.

# M5 wave B part 1 execution: task 5 (owner-approved, 2026-08-07)

Task 5 of the wave B plan: three test gaps in the corpus-admission checker closed, plus one schema rule.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 154 | `tests/helpers.py`'s `make_task_spec` gains a `variants` override: a list of `{id, patch, label, hack_category}` dicts, validated through `VariantSpec.model_validate` and swapped onto `evaluation.variants` wholesale via `model_copy`, the same pattern the existing `allowed_paths`/`seed`/`acceptance_suite` overrides already use. This is the mechanical blocker that kept invariant 6 off the fast path: the fixture spec (`tests/fixtures/specs/valid-task.yaml`) carries exactly one variant, `gold`, labeled clean, so no `ScriptedRunner` test could script a hacked tree at all before this change. `test_seedcheck.py`'s `run_check_with_outcomes` (the helper every quarantine test in that file already calls) gains a passthrough `variants` keyword and a `**hack_outcomes` catch-all: each hacked variant's own outcome map is a `hack_<id>` keyword (`hack_h5={...}` for a variant `id: h5`), scripted onto the `hack-<id>` tree `check_task`'s invariant 6 materializes, matching the `pristine`/`seeded`/`gold-<id>` key shapes `ScriptedRunner` already understands. Two new tests ride this: `test_quarantined_failure_does_not_break_hacked_variants_green` pins `check_task`'s existing `_drop_quarantined(hack, spec.seed.quarantine)` call at the invariant 6 site (`seedcheck.py:289`), the fourth member of the quarantine-exclusion set documented at `test_seedcheck.py:199-203` and the only one with no test until now; `test_acceptance_matrix_must_pass_tree_red_fails_the_invariant` pins the `must_pass_on` loop's red branch (`seedcheck.py:332-336`), left unexercised by all four pre-existing acceptance-matrix tests. Neither test needed a production change: both pin behavior the code already had. | Closes two of the three live gaps the wave A final review's ledger triage flagged. Invariant 6's gap is not hypothetical: `tasks/rich-0001.yaml` ships a real quarantine entry (`tests/test_console.py::test_brokenpipeerror`) alongside two hacked variants, so a quarantined flake going red on a hacked tree would have blocked that task's re-admission, with no test standing between a future edit and deleting the `_drop_quarantined` call while the suite stayed green. Verified by deletion rather than by inspection: replacing the call with `hack_view = hack` failed the new test with `hacked-variants-green` reporting `h5 still red: ['tests/t.py::test_flaky']`; restoring the call returned the suite to green. The invariant 7 gap is the same shape one invariant over: the four pre-existing acceptance-matrix tests only ever drive the `must_fail_on` side red or leave both sides green, so the `if red: ok7 = False` branch on the pass side had never run under test; gating it off with `if False and red:` failed the new test with the invariant reporting `ok=True` where the test expects `not item.ok`, confirming the branch the test now reaches. | A single `hacked: dict[str, dict[str, str]]` keyword (`hacked={"h5": {...}}`) on `run_check_with_outcomes` instead of `**hack_outcomes`: rejected, the brief's own test body calls the helper as `hack_h5={...}`, and matching that call shape outweighs a marginally more explicit dict-of-dicts. Letting `make_task_spec`'s `variants` override pass raw dicts straight into `model_copy` with no `VariantSpec.model_validate` step: rejected, the field is typed `list[VariantSpec]` and `model_copy` performs no validation on its own, so an unvalidated list of dicts would leave `v.label`/`v.id` attribute access broken the first time `check_task` read one. |
| 155 | `TaskSpec._acceptance_names_resolve` (`skeptic/spec.py:206-233`) gains a third check, appended after the existing `seeded`-in-`must_fail_on` raise: `both = sorted(set(must_pass_on) & set(must_fail_on))`, raising `ValueError` naming the overlapping ids when non-empty. The existing unknown-name check two lines above unions `must_pass_on` and `must_fail_on` before checking membership (`names = set(...) \| set(...)`), so a name present in both lists was never caught there: it loads cleanly, and `check_task`'s invariant 7 (`seedcheck.py:332-341`) then resolves and materializes that tree twice, once per loop, with one branch guaranteed to fail by construction, reporting a corpus authoring mistake as an invariant failure instead of a spec-load error. `test_acceptance_suite_rejects_a_name_in_both_lists` (`tests/test_spec.py`) pins it: verified by removing the new block, the test failed with `Failed: DID NOT RAISE ValidationError`; restoring the block returned the pass. The rule is written as its own appended `if both: raise` block rather than folded into the unknown-name check, so task 7's planned further rule (`must_fail_on` must list every hacked variant) can land as another appended block in the same method without touching this one. | Closes the third gap from the wave A final review's ledger triage. `must_pass_on` and `must_fail_on` partition the acceptance matrix's trees into two claims, a suite that must stay green and a suite that must go red; a name on both lists is an authoring mistake rather than a third valid claim, and admission should refuse it at spec load rather than let it surface as a confusing invariant-7 failure during a real check run. | Silently preferring one list on a tie (`must_fail_on` wins) instead of raising: rejected, that hides the authoring mistake instead of naming it, against a one-line YAML fix once named. Folding the both-lists check into the existing unknown-name block as a combined condition: rejected, the two are separate failure modes with separate messages, and a shared `if` would blur which problem a given task file has. |
| 156 | `run_check_with_outcomes`'s `hack_<id>` handling (row 154) gains a crosscheck against the spec's own declared hacked-variant ids. After `make_task_spec` builds the spec, `declared_hacked = sorted(v.id for v in spec.evaluation.variants if v.label == "hacked")`; any `hack_<id>` keyword whose id is not in that set now raises `TypeError` naming the bad id and the declared ids, before the outcome map ever reaches `ScriptedRunner`'s dict. The reverse direction stays unchecked on purpose: a declared hacked variant with no scripted outcome still defaults to a green suite through `ScriptedRunner`'s own default-on-missing-key behavior, which is the pass side every invariant-6 test above already relies on; the helper's docstring now states that default explicitly instead of leaving it implicit. `test_hack_outcome_for_an_undeclared_variant_id_is_rejected` pins the new raise, matched on the bad id (`h7`) against a spec declaring only `h5` hacked. | Same-day coordinator review of task 5's own commit (`3ee4429`): a `hack_h7={...}` kwarg against a spec declaring only `h5` silently became an unread entry in the outcomes dict, the real `hack-h5` tree fell through to `ScriptedRunner`'s green default, and `hacked-variants-green` passed having pinned nothing. A typo'd or stale id in a later wave-B test would fail the same way with no signal, in helper code every later invariant-6 test rides on, which is the exact silent-pass shape task 5's own three tests exist to close. | Restructuring `run_check_with_outcomes` off the `hack_<id>` keyword-prefix shape into a `hacked: dict[str, dict[str, str]]` parameter: reviewer ruled against it, since a plain dict parameter carries the identical typo/staleness gap and the prefix shape already matches the file's own keyword-argument idiom (`pristine_first`, `gold`, `hack_h5`); the fix is the orthogonal crosscheck rather than a reshape. Checking the reverse direction too (raising when a declared hacked variant has no scripted outcome): rejected, that default is exactly what lets a caller name only the tree its own test cares about, the same convention `pristine_first`/`seeded`/`gold` already use, and every existing invariant-6 test depends on an unscripted declared variant reading green. |

**Ripple.** The brief's own combined invocation, `python -m pytest -q tests/test_seedcheck.py -k "hacked_variants_green or must_pass_tree_red" tests/test_spec.py -k both_lists`, hits the same pytest quirk task 3's row already recorded: pytest keeps only the last `-k` given for the whole session, so it collects 53 items across both files and runs only the one matching `both_lists`. The two selections were run separately instead. `python -m pytest -q tests/test_seedcheck.py -k "hacked_variants_green or must_pass_tree_red"`: 2 passed, 24 deselected. `python -m pytest -q tests/test_spec.py -k both_lists`: 1 passed, 26 deselected. Break-and-revert evidence for all three tests, each reverted immediately after capture: deleting `_drop_quarantined(hack, spec.seed.quarantine)` at the invariant 6 site (replaced with `hack_view = hack`) failed `test_quarantined_failure_does_not_break_hacked_variants_green` with `assert False` off `InvariantResult(name='hacked-variants-green', ok=False, detail="h5 still red: ['tests/t.py::test_flaky']")`; gating the `must_pass_on` loop's red branch off (`if False and red:`) failed `test_acceptance_matrix_must_pass_tree_red_fails_the_invariant` with `assert (not True)` off an `ok=True` result reading `"pass on ['pristine', 'gold'], fail on ['seeded']"`; removing the new both-lists block from `_acceptance_names_resolve` failed `test_acceptance_suite_rejects_a_name_in_both_lists` with `Failed: DID NOT RAISE ValidationError`. Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 614 passed, 81 deselected (+3 over the pre-task baseline of 611, the three new tests); `ruff check .` clean.

**Review fix.** Coordinator review of task 5's initial commit (`3ee4429`) found the `hack_<id>` crosscheck gap row 156 describes. Break-and-revert evidence: `test_hack_outcome_for_an_undeclared_variant_id_is_rejected` (the new test) failed against the pre-fix helper with `Failed: DID NOT RAISE TypeError`; adding the crosscheck returned it to green. Covering tests after the fix, run together: `python -m pytest -q tests/test_seedcheck.py -k "undeclared_variant_id or hacked_variants_green or must_pass_tree_red"`: 3 passed, 24 deselected. `python -m pytest -q tests/test_spec.py -k both_lists`: 1 passed, 26 deselected (unaffected by this round, re-run for completeness). Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 615 passed, 81 deselected (+1 over this task's own prior baseline of 614, the one new test); `ruff check .` clean.

# M5 wave B part 1 execution: task 6 (owner-approved, 2026-08-07)

Task 6 of the wave B plan: four test-only pins over acceptance-ladder and
evalkit behavior that is correct today and unprotected, plus a ruling on two
shared test constants. Zero production changes; this task's brief scoped it
that way and no pin caught a real defect.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 157 | Four pins added, none touching `skeptic/`. (1) `test_advtests_io_persists_when_the_ladder_dies` (`tests/test_cli_verify.py`) fakes `cli.observe_advtests` to raise `SkepticInfraError` on a would-be-PASS paid run and asserts `t2_advtests_io.json` still exists with a non-empty `responses` list after the run reports INFRA_ERROR (exit 3): the write at `cli.py:741-743` sits before the `observe_advtests` call on purpose (row 143), and the only existing artifact test (`test_paid_judge_io_artifact_persists_request_and_response`) fakes `observe_advtests` to a canned success, so it cannot tell a write-before from a write-after. (2) `test_rejected_at_names_the_first_rung_not_the_last` (`tests/test_collector.py`) builds a candidate that fails both `target_coverage` (no changed-file line ran under a test context) and `seeded_green` (passed the seeded tree, non-discriminating) and asserts `rejected_at == "target_coverage"`, pinning `_ADV_RUNG_ORDER`'s middle two entries (`collector.py:1928`), which only had `reference`-before-the-rest covered before this. (3) `test_yield_detail_lists_rungs_in_ladder_order` (`tests/test_t2_advtests.py`) builds a zero-trusted report with one candidate rejected at `seeded_green` and one at `reference` and asserts `"at reference"` appears before `"at seeded_green"` in the rendered detail string, pinning `t2_advtests.py`'s `_RUNGS` ordering (lines 61-64), which drives the shipped "rejected N at \<rung\>, ..." text and had no test of its own order. (4) `test_judge_alone_drops_a_clean_row_without_judge_data` (`tests/test_evalkit.py`) builds six rows where `gold-prime` (a clean row) carries `judge_flagged=None` and every hacked row carries a real value, then asserts `render_table`'s drop line reads `"1 gold-prime"` and contains `"no judge data"`, pinning the `dropped_fp` half of `render_table`'s drop-count branch (`evalkit.py:446-449`): the existing dropped-count test's only `judge_flagged=None` row is `h5`, a hacked row, so `dropped_fp` (keyed off clean-row variants) was always empty there and its own fold never ran against a real drop. Also added to `test_generate_candidates_returns_io_dict` (`tests/test_testgen.py`): `entry["in_tok"] == 100`, `entry["out_tok"] == 50`, and `entry["text"]` assertions on the same response object the test already builds, closing the gap that nothing pinned those three fields being read off the real response rather than fabricated. | Each pin protects a number an eval table or a debugging session actually reads: the dead-ladder artifact is the only evidence of why a paid run produced nothing; `rejected_at` is the yield stat every eval table reports; the `_RUNGS` order is the literal text of that stat's detail string; the judge-alone drop line is what tells a table reader a baseline's denominator shrank and by which variant. All four were currently correct and could be broken today with the full suite staying green: that gap between correct and covered is what the brief calls "unprotected". | Following the brief's literal test bodies (`observe_advtests_with`, `by_id`, `pair_with_rejections` helpers) verbatim: none of the three exist in either `tests/test_collector.py` or `tests/test_t2_advtests.py`; both files' own conventions (`_advtest_fake_runs` plus `make_observed_pair` in the first, `_candidate`/`_pair_with_advtests` in the second) were used instead, since inventing the brief's named helpers fresh would duplicate machinery the files already have. |
| 158 | `SPEC_N2`/`SPEC_N8` (`tests/test_testgen.py:112-113`), two module-level `TaskSpec` instances built by `_spec_with_n_candidates` and shared by six `generate_candidates` tests, are replaced with an inline `_spec_with_n_candidates(n)` call at each of the six call sites; the two module-level names are deleted. Nothing mutates either constant today (`_Model` has no `frozen=True`, so pydantic would allow it, but no test does), so this closes a class of future bug rather than a live one: a hypothetical test that mutated the shared spec via `model_copy`'s own escape hatches or direct attribute assignment would silently change `n_candidates` for every test running after it in file order. | The brief's own preference: "costs one call per test and removes the class of bug entirely" against sharing two names for a construction that costs nothing to repeat. `_spec_with_n_candidates` was already a factory function before this task; the module-level constants were the only thing standing between "shared, mutable, and never mutated" and "not shared, so not mutable by construction." | Leaving `SPEC_N2`/`SPEC_N8` in place with a comment naming the hazard, the brief's own fallback: rejected, since the fix is six one-line call-site edits with no behavior change, cheaper than a comment a future editor has to keep reading and keep believing. |

**Ripple.** All four break-and-revert pairs below were captured with `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q <path> -k <selector>`, one selector per pin (the branch's own recorded pytest quirk: two `-k` flags in one invocation keep only the last, so the brief's combined selector was not used).

Pin 1, `tests/test_cli_verify.py -k ladder_dies`: GREEN before any change (1 passed). Break: moved the `t2_advtests_io.json` write in `cli.py` to after the `observe_advtests` call. RED: `AssertionError: assert False` off `io_path.is_file()`, the artifact never written since the exception fires before the moved write executes. Reverted; GREEN again (1 passed), confirmed by `git diff --stat skeptic/cli.py` showing no diff.

Pin 2, `tests/test_collector.py -k first_rung`: GREEN before any change (1 passed). Break: swapped `_ADV_RUNG_ORDER` to `("reference", "seeded_green", "target_coverage", "gold_prime")`. RED: `AssertionError: assert 'seeded_green' == 'target_coverage'`. Reverted; GREEN again (1 passed), `git diff --stat skeptic/collector.py` clean.

Pin 3, `tests/test_t2_advtests.py -k ladder_order`: GREEN before any change (1 passed). Break: reordered `_RUNGS` to `("generation", "import_screen", "seeded_green", "target_coverage", "reference", "gold_prime")`. RED: `AssertionError: assert 70 < 51` off a detail string reading `"...rejected 1 at seeded_green, 1 at reference"`. Reverted; GREEN again (1 passed), `git diff --stat skeptic/checks/t2_advtests.py` clean.

Pin 4, `tests/test_evalkit.py -k drops_a_clean_row`: GREEN before any change (1 passed), and the existing `test_render_table_prints_dropped_count_for_judge_alone_baseline` stayed GREEN throughout, confirming the two tests exercise different code paths. Break: `render_table`'s `dropped_fp` comprehension changed to iterate `b.false_positives.items()` instead of `fps.items()`, so a variant absent from the (already-dropped) baseline dict never gets a denominator to compare against. RED: `AssertionError: assert '1 gold-prime' in '...| judge-alone | 2/3 | 0/3 | gold 0/1 |'`, the drop line missing entirely since `dropped_det` stayed 0 and `dropped_fp` came back empty under the bug. The pre-existing dropped-count test still passed under this same break (`1 passed`), the exact "existing coverage misses this" case the pin exists to close. Reverted; both tests GREEN again (2 passed), `git diff --stat skeptic/evalkit.py` clean.

Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`) after all edits: 619 passed, 81 deselected (+4 over the pre-task baseline of 615, the four new pin tests; the io-dict assertions and the SPEC_N2/N8 factory conversion added no new test functions). `ruff check .`: clean. `git diff --stat skeptic/`: empty, confirming the zero-production-change constraint held.

# M5 wave B part 1 execution: task 7 (owner-approved, 2026-08-07)

Task 7 of the wave B plan: the h6 overlap fix, generalized into a schema rule so it is no longer a coincidence.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 159 | `TaskSpec._acceptance_names_resolve` (`skeptic/spec.py:206-246`) gains a fourth check, appended after the both-lists raise so it only evaluates names that already resolved: `hacked = {v.id for v in self.evaluation.variants if v.label == "hacked"}`, `missing = sorted(hacked - set(must_fail_on))`, raising `ValueError` naming every missing id when non-empty. A hack is by construction a patch that does not fix the bug; a patch that does fix it is a gold-prime. The acceptance suite is by construction the frozen statement of the bug being fixed, so the suite must be red on every hacked variant or it fails to discriminate the bug it exists to pin. Eval B's GREEN-wrong classifier (task 17) rests on that exact premise: a hack that stays GREEN on the suite makes the classifier's output mean nothing. `rich-0001`'s h6 hack is the coincidence this closes: it special-cases `required_space = 4` at widths `{3, 4, 5, 16}`, exactly the seeded tree's four failing-test widths, and the acceptance suite's first test also renders at width 16, so it passes on the h6 tree. Only the width-14 test (added for a different reason) catches h6. Nothing before this rule would have noticed if a future edit retuned width 14 or sharpened h6 to include it; the suite would go green on the hack and admission would stay silent. The moment the validator landed, both real task yamls failed to load, since neither listed its hacked variants in `must_fail_on`: `tasks/click-0001.yaml:71` and `tasks/rich-0001.yaml:98` both add their two hack ids (`[seeded, h5, h1]` and `[seeded, h6, h3]`). `acceptance/rich-0001/test_acceptance.py` gains a comment above the width-16 test naming the coincidence and stating both widths are load-bearing, so must_fail_on listing h6 now fails admission rather than going quiet if either width is dropped or retuned. `test_acceptance_suite_must_fail_on_every_hacked_variant` (`tests/test_spec.py`) pins the rule with the brief's own fixture: a `gold`/`h5` spec whose `must_fail_on` lists only `seeded`. | Closes the exact gap the brief names: a hack passing the suite is either a corpus bug (the suite is too weak) or a labeling bug (the variant is mislabeled), and both should surface at spec load rather than as a quiet pass three invariants later. The validator, both yaml edits, and the new test land in one commit on purpose: landing the validator alone breaks every fast-suite test that loads either real task yaml. | A comment in the acceptance file naming which widths matter, with no schema enforcement: rejected, a comment pins nothing, since nothing stops a later edit from retuning width 14 or sharpening h6 and the suite would go green with no test noticing. |

**Live admission.** Both corpus tasks re-admitted clean against the new `must_fail_on` lists: seven invariants PASS each, and neither hack came back green on the acceptance suite (the failure mode step 5 says to stop and escalate on). `acceptance-matrix` reports `pass on ['pristine', 'gold', 'gold-prime'], fail on ['seeded', 'h5', 'h1']` for click-0001 and `pass on ['pristine', 'gold', 'gold-prime'], fail on ['seeded', 'h6', 'h3']` for rich-0001: three pass-side and three fail-side trees per task, up from three pass-side and one fail-side before this task added each task's two hack ids to `must_fail_on`.

`skeptic seed --task click-0001 --check`: 35.972s total wall-clock (23.33s user, 5.17s system, 79% cpu). `skeptic seed --task rich-0001 --check`: 53.751s total wall-clock (21.88s user, 5.41s system, 50% cpu). Both measured with `time` against `./.venv/bin/skeptic`. One hacked-variant acceptance run was added per hack (two hacks per task), so the acceptance-matrix invariant went from 4 to 6 runs per task: one fresh materialize, patch, copytree, and full pytest run each. Part 2's corpus lane budgets ten more tasks against these two numbers: at roughly 35-55s per task for the acceptance-matrix's added cost alone, ten tasks add on the order of 6-9 minutes of admission wall-clock beyond what a suite-less admission would cost, before counting the other six invariants each task also runs.

Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 620 passed, 81 deselected (+1 over the pre-task baseline of 619, the one new test). `ruff check .`: clean.
