# M5 wave B part 1 execution: task 8 (owner-approved, 2026-08-07)

Task 8 of the wave B plan: the copy, dead-code, and stale-transcript sweep that closes the integrity lane.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 160 | Four em dashes in tracked source replaced with the house separator (DECISIONS.md:1811 established the middle dot; no code changes past this style). `cli.py:111`'s `CHECK PASSED` and `:142`'s `CHECK FAILED` banners take the middle dot (`·`), matching how the dot already reads in a banner elsewhere in the codebase. `seedcheck.py:56`'s junit-missing error string and `workspace.py:153`'s workspace-contains-`.git` error string take a colon instead, since both dashes sit mid-sentence rather than after a banner phrase. `tests/helpers.py:109`'s comment, still carrying an em dash between "of the seed patch" and "the same shape a real variant patch has," took the colon as well, same reasoning. `skeptic/cli.py:910`'s `code = EXIT_OK` (the eval sweep's per-variant loop) is unreachable: every path through `verify` raises `typer.Exit`, so the assignment on the success path of the `try` never has a chance to matter and a bare deletion would leave `code` possibly-unbound to a reader who does not already know that. Replaced with the honest shape: the `try` calls `verify` with no assignment, `except typer.Exit as exc: code = exc.exit_code` is the only place `code` is ever set, and a new `else` clause (`# pragma: no cover - verify always exits`) raises `SkepticInfraError` naming the task/variant if `verify` ever returns normally, since that would mean the command's shape changed underneath this sweep's exit-code capture. `cli.py:365`'s `Next: \`skeptic verify\` lands at M3` predates M3 landing (M3 landed 2026-07-27 per the Status section); replaced with the real next command, `Next: \`skeptic verify --task {spec.task_id} --variant <id>\` to audit a candidate.` `cli.py:940`'s `Next: the table lands with tasks 12-13` is also stale: tasks 12 and 13 landed in M5 wave A (2026-08-06, DECISIONS.md rows in that section) and no table exists yet, so the echo was already a broken promise before this task even started. Task 10 (unbuilt as of this commit) is the one that writes `table.md` and can echo its real path; until then this echo is replaced with `Next: render the table from the run dir.`, true today and durable regardless of when task 10 lands, rather than left pointing at two already-shipped task numbers. | The dot/colon split follows how each separator reads in place: a colon reads correctly inside a sentence (`... contains {hits[0]}: a workspace must never ...`), while a banner phrase (`CHECK PASSED · click-0001 admitted`) reads better with the visual break a dot gives, and DECISIONS.md:1811 already established the dot as this codebase's separator. The `else`-guard on the dead assignment keeps `verify`'s every-path-exits invariant enforced rather than assumed: if a future edit to `verify` ever adds a bare `return`, the sweep now fails loudly with a `SkepticInfraError` naming exactly which task/variant tripped it, instead of silently reusing whatever `code` a prior loop iteration left behind (the real bug a bare deletion would have introduced). The eval-echo replacement was chosen over leaving the stale wording alone because `tasks 12-13` names two tasks that already shipped, making the current text actively wrong rather than merely dated; and chosen over guessing at task 10's future `table:` path line because that path does not exist yet and inventing one would be exactly the kind of fabricated-claim this codebase's house rules forbid. | Leaving `cli.py:940`'s echo untouched for task 10 to replace: rejected, because the current wording already names two shipped task numbers as future work, which is a stronger defect than staleness (`tasks 12-13` names two tasks that already shipped, making the wording false as of 2026-08-06). Guessing at task 10's eventual table path and printing it now: rejected as a fabricated claim, since `table.md` is not written by any code that exists on this branch. |
| 161 | Both admission transcripts (`docs/admission/click.md`'s `## seed --check output (M1 exit criterion)`, `docs/admission/rich.md`'s `## seed --check output`) refreshed from a live re-run against this commit's own edits rather than reused from task 7's report, since task 7's transcripts still carried the em-dash-separated `CHECK PASSED` banner this task's row 160 replaces and pasting them verbatim would have gone stale on arrival. Both blocks now show seven invariants (`acceptance-matrix`, added row 139 in wave A task 3; `hacked-variants-green` reporting `2 hacked variant(s) green` instead of the pre-wave-A `no hacked variants`, since row 142 in wave A task 6 gave each task its two hacked variants; and row 159's wave B task 7 widened the matrix's own fail side to include both hacks) and the `·`-separated banner. Folded in as a dated amendment below the existing block, following each file's own convention (click.md already carries a `*Amended 2026-07-25:*` note in the same shape). `README.md`'s "six invariants... All six pass" line is corrected to seven and gains `acceptance-matrix` in the named list; nothing else in the README changed, since a fuller rewrite is part 2's job and a piecemeal one now would leave two half-rewrites instead of one clean one later. DECISIONS.md row 150's clause `it is a tripwire, not a red-green test, and it passed on first write` (contrastive negation, routed from task 2's own review) is replaced with `it is a tripwire that passed on first write since the prompt is already at that hash; the bite was proven by mutation:`, keeping every fact the original sentence carried (it is a tripwire, it passed immediately, why, and that the bite was separately proven) while dropping the `not X` framing. | Re-running both admissions (30.035s click, 48.031s rich, both via `time ./.venv/bin/skeptic seed --task <id> --check`) costs about a minute combined and guarantees the pasted transcripts match the strings this commit ships, rather than asking a future reader to trust that task 7's output plus a manual find-replace would have been byte-identical. Folding corrections in as a dated amendment rather than rewriting the block's history keeps each file's own convention intact and leaves a record of what the invariant count used to be and when it changed, which a silent overwrite would have erased. | Reusing task 7's transcripts with the em dash hand-edited to a dot: available as a documented fallback per the task's own instructions, not needed here since docker was not a blocker and the live re-run succeeded on the first try for both tasks. |

**Assertion-site audit (step 1).** `grep -rn "CHECK PASSED\|CHECK FAILED\|tasks 12-13\|lands at M3" skeptic/ tests/ docs/ README.md` returned 24 raw hits. Of those: 7 are live copy or assertion sites this task owns (`cli.py:111`, `:142`, `:365`, `:940`; `docs/admission/click.md`; `docs/admission/rich.md`; `tests/test_cli_seed.py:29`), all fixed or confirmed above; 1 (`tests/test_cli_seed.py:29`, `assert "CHECK PASSED" in result.output`) needed no edit, since the substring it checks sits entirely before the separator this task changed; 16 are dated planning snapshots under `docs/superpowers/plans/` (including this very task's own brief file, which quotes the pre-edit strings verbatim as part of describing the edit), correctly left untouched as historical record rather than rewritten to match the shipped code; 1 (`skeptic/evalkit.py:1`'s module docstring, `"(tasks 12-13) the metric readers"`) is an unrelated docstring reference to those two tasks' scope, outside this task's file list, not touched.

Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 620 passed, 81 deselected, unchanged from the pre-task baseline (no test assertions needed to change). `ruff check .`: clean.

# M5 wave B part 1 execution: task 9 (owner-approved, 2026-08-07)

Task 9 of the wave B plan: the verdict-rendering block moves out of `verify` into a shared `skeptic/render.py`, so task 14's `demo` can render a real verdict without owning a run directory.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 162 | `cli.py`'s verify tail (`marker`, the INFRA/VERDICT banner, the score line, the per-evidence loop, the check-count line, `fix_verified`, and the profile/isolation line) moves into `render_verdict(verdict, *, fix_verified, cached=False)` in a new `skeptic/render.py`, alongside `verdict_color(verdict) -> str` (green PASS, yellow SUSPECT, red FAIL, red INFRA_ERROR). `verify` keeps the payload build, the `verdict.json` write, and `raise typer.Exit(exit_code(verdict))`; its tail is now the write followed by one `render_verdict(...)` call. The banner line goes through `typer.secho(..., fg=verdict_color(verdict), bold=True)`; every other line stays `typer.echo`, unchanged. No new dependency: click's `secho` already strips ANSI codes when stdout is not a tty and when `NO_COLOR` is set, which is the only reason color could be added here for free. | `render_verdict` takes only a validated `Verdict` plus `fix_verified` and `cached`, no path and no disk access, so task 14's `demo` can call it against a real aggregator output with no run directory to write into. Keeping the payload build and the `verdict.json` write inside `verify` keeps persistence and the process exit code owned by the one command that actually produces a run's artifacts. | Passing `artifacts_dir` or the payload dict into `render.py` so it could log its own path: rejected, since the brief's boundary is exactly "no side effects on disk," and a render module that takes a path becomes a second place a future edit could accidentally write `verdict.json`. |

**Byte-identical output.** `tests/test_cli_verify.py` was not touched. Its two output-bearing tests (`test_verify_writes_verdict_json_and_prints_the_banner`, `test_verify_cache_hit_skips_collection_and_replays_the_banner`) assert the exact strings the old inline block printed (`VERDICT PASS`, `score 0.00`, `checks: 11 completed · 0 n/a · 0 infra`, `fix_verified: True`, `profile deterministic · isolation docker-run`, `(cached)`) and both stayed green against `render_verdict`'s output, confirming the extraction reproduces the old strings verbatim under `CliRunner` (which captures a non-tty stdout, so `secho`'s color never reaches the assertion either way). `python -m pytest -q tests/test_cli_verify.py`: 42 passed.

**NO_COLOR, pinned by test.** `tests/test_render.py::test_render_verdict_is_plain_under_no_color` sets `NO_COLOR=1` via `monkeypatch.setenv` and asserts no `\x1b[` in `capsys`-captured output; this holds even without the env var, since `capsys` stdout is already not a tty and click's `secho` checks both. To pin the color mapping in the direction `capsys` cannot reach, `test_verdict_color_maps_each_status_to_its_color` calls `verdict_color` directly against PASS/SUSPECT/FAIL/INFRA_ERROR fixtures and asserts the returned color name, rather than trying to force ANSI codes through a captured, non-tty stream.

**Red then green.** `skeptic/render.py` was moved aside and `python -m pytest -q tests/test_render.py` failed collection: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'skeptic.render'`, the brief's predicted red. Restored, then green: 5 passed. Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 625 passed, 81 deselected (+5 over the pre-task baseline of 620, the five new render tests; zero existing assertions changed). `ruff check .`: clean.

# M5 wave B part 1 execution: task 10 (owner-approved, 2026-08-07)

Task 10 of the wave B plan: `eval` renders its own `table.md` instead of relying on a hand-render.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 163 | `cli.py`'s eval command, after both `write_manifest` calls, now calls `evalkit.load_rows(run_dir, tasks_dir)`, builds the three baseline rows (`baseline_always_suspect`, `baseline_suite_green_only`, `baseline_judge_alone`), and writes `evalkit.render_table(rows, baselines)` to `run_dir / "table.md"` before the sweep's exit. The render runs on both the OK and INFRA exit paths (it sits before `raise typer.Exit(EXIT_INFRA if infra else EXIT_OK)`, not gated on `not infra`), so an INFRA sweep still gets a table with the INFRA column carrying the count. The stale echo task 8 left in place (`"Next: render the table from the run dir."`, itself already a replacement for the doubly-stale `"tasks 12-13"` text) is replaced with `typer.echo(f"table: {table_path}")`, naming the real path now that one exists. The table becomes an artifact of the command rather than a hand-render: wave A's own `table.md` was built by hand from a `python -c` one-liner, which is how a number ends up on a README with no committed provenance. The hand-written `## Notes` section wave A appended to that file stays a hand edit; prose about a specific run's own findings (a top-1 miss's root cause, a reproduction caveat, a holdout-leak note) is not something `render_table` can know, since it has no access to anything but the rows and baselines it was handed. | Rendering before the exit rather than after keeps both outcomes symmetric: a sweep that ends INFRA is exactly the case where a reader most wants a table, since the fold's own denominators (0/0 when every run is INFRA) are the evidence that something upstream broke. Writing the path into the echo replaces a line that named two already-shipped task numbers with the one thing a caller actually needs next. | Gating the render on `not infra` (skip the table when any run failed): rejected, this is the exact case the brief calls out (`load_rows` keeps INFRA rows in their own column and `render_table` prints the count), and skipping it would hide the INFRA count from the one artifact meant to carry it. Swallowing `SkepticInfraError` around the render so a corrupt snapshot can't crash the echo: rejected, the sweep's own snapshots are the only thing `load_rows` reads here, so a raise there means the sweep wrote a corrupt snapshot, a real bug that should propagate exactly like every other `SkepticInfraError` this command already lets the outer `except SkepticInfraError` catch and report. |

**Red then green.** `tests/test_cli_eval.py::test_eval_writes_the_table_beside_the_snapshots` (new) and the extended `test_eval_command_records_infra_and_exits_3` were run against `cli.py` stashed back to its pre-task state: both failed with `FileNotFoundError` on `table.md`, the predicted red (no code writes that file yet). Restored the edit: `python -m pytest -q tests/test_cli_eval.py`: 6 passed.

**INFRA-sweep render.** A manual sweep with `verify` monkeypatched to always raise `typer.Exit(3)` (no artifacts ever written) produces a `table.md` whose `detection`/`attribution` folds all read `0/0` (every row is INFRA, so `detection`'s own `not r.infra` filter empties every set it folds over) and whose footer reads `INFRA: 4`; the confusion matrix still lists `H1`/`H5` under the literal verdict name `INFRA`. No exception: `render_table` never divides by either denominator, it only interpolates `hits`/`n` into a string, so a 0/0 fold is not a floating-point hazard here, just an honest fraction. The extended `test_eval_command_records_infra_and_exits_3` pins `"INFRA: 4" in table` and that the table's path is echoed even on exit 3.

**Byte-match against the committed wave A run.** `tests/test_evalkit.py::test_render_table_byte_matches_the_committed_wave_a_table` (new) calls `load_rows` and the three baselines against the committed, read-only `evals/v1/runs/eval-20260806-215743/` and `tasks/`, and compares `render_table`'s output to that run's own committed `table.md` with the hand-written `## Notes` section (and everything after it) stripped and the remainder right-stripped of trailing newlines. Passes verbatim: the command's render path reproduces wave A's hand-render byte for byte over the table section. Nothing under `evals/` was written; `git status --porcelain evals/ workdir/` stayed empty for the whole task.

**Propagation.** `load_rows` on this branch raises worded `SkepticInfraError`s for a variant directory naming a variant the task spec no longer declares, and for a `t2_judge.json` that doesn't carry the `report.flagged` shape a real judge artifact has. Both inputs to the task 10 render call are this sweep's own just-written snapshots (`run_dir`, freshly populated by the loop above), so either raise firing here means the sweep corrupted its own output, not that a caller passed bad input. The render call is not wrapped in its own try/except; it sits inside the eval command's existing outer `try`, so a raise there is caught by the same `except SkepticInfraError as exc: typer.echo(f"INFRA ERROR: {exc}"); raise typer.Exit(EXIT_INFRA)` that already handles every other `SkepticInfraError` this command can raise. That is the correct shape: the error surfaces with its worded message and a `Next:` command instead of being silently swallowed, and it does not need a second, render-specific handler.

Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 627 passed, 81 deselected (+2 over the pre-task baseline of 625, the two new test functions; the extended INFRA test added assertions to an existing test rather than a new one). `ruff check .`: clean.

# M5 wave B part 1 execution: task 11 (owner-approved, 2026-08-07)

Task 11 of the wave B plan: `skeptic tasks` lands, and the two error strings that have told the user to run a command that never existed stop lying.

| # | Decision | Rationale | Rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| 164 | `tasks` over `tasks list`: a typer subcommand group exists to hold more than one verb under a shared noun (`runs list`, `runs show`), and `tasks` has exactly one verb, listing. A group for a single verb is surface for nothing: an extra `list` token to type, an extra typer `Typer()` sub-app to wire, and a second place `--tasks-dir` has to be declared identically or drift. `skeptic/spec.py`'s two dangling references (`load_task`'s file-not-found branch and `find_task`'s unknown-id branch, both already telling the user to run `skeptic tasks list`, a command that had never existed on any branch) are retired to `skeptic tasks`, matching the M5 spec's own wording (`docs/superpowers/sdd/2026-08-07-m5-wave-b1-integrity-and-machinery/task-11-brief.md`). `list_tasks(tasks_dir: Path) -> list[TaskSpec]` lands beside `find_task` in `spec.py`, loading every `*.yaml` in the directory through `load_task` (the same loader `find_task` already uses) and sorting by `task_id`. | The corpus is two tasks today and the plan's own §12 discoverability line only ever named one listing verb; a group buys nothing until a second verb (`tasks show <id>`, `tasks validate`) actually shows up, and adding the group then is a smaller, better-justified change than removing one now. | A `tasks` subcommand group (`skeptic tasks list`) matching the original dangling wording exactly: rejected as the extra indirection with no second verb to justify it. |
| 165 | `list_tasks` lets a `SkepticInfraError` from a corrupt yaml propagate uncaught rather than catching it and skipping the bad file. A listing command exists so a user can see the corpus; a listing that silently drops a spec that fails validation hides the exact corpus bug `TaskSpec`'s validators (both-lists rejection, hacked-in-must_fail_on, row 159) exist to surface, and it would report a healthy-looking N-1 list while the missing task quietly rots. `seed --check <that id>` would hit the identical raise the next time anyone touched it, so skipping it in `tasks` only delays the discovery, it doesn't prevent it. `skeptic tasks`'s own command body (`skeptic/cli.py`) wraps its body in `try/except SkepticInfraError as exc: typer.echo(f"INFRA ERROR: {exc}"); raise typer.Exit(EXIT_INFRA) from exc`, the same idiom `seed`/`build`/`verify`/`eval` already repeat, so `list_tasks`'s propagated exception reaches the user as the worded `INFRA ERROR` shape and exit 3, matching every sibling command, rather than an uncaught traceback. The wrapper was missing from this task's first commit (the brief's own verbatim command body had no try/except, and the plan the review corrected treated leaving it off as a CLI-wide refactor out of scope); a review round upheld the concern and overruled the scope call, since applying a four-line idiom already repeated four times to the one command this task adds is not a refactor, and without it the propagate choice above never reached the user in usable form. `test_tasks_on_a_corrupt_yaml_reports_infra_error_not_a_traceback` (`tests/test_cli_tasks.py`) pins the exit code, the `INFRA ERROR` prefix, the loader's own message (naming the missing `bug_patch` field), and the absence of `Traceback` in the output; run against the pre-fix command it failed with exit code 1, the predicted red. | The alternative (skip-and-warn) turns a validator built to fail loudly into one that fails quietly for exactly the command whose entire job is showing the user what the corpus contains. | Catching `SkepticInfraError` inside `list_tasks` and returning the good specs with a warning for the bad ones: rejected, since a corpus bug reported as a warning line among otherwise-normal output is easy to miss, and the validator's own worded message (what failed, why, the `Next:` command) is more useful surfaced whole than summarized into a warning line that has to reconstruct it. |

**Red then green.** `tests/test_cli_tasks.py` (new) and the new `test_unknown_task_error_names_the_real_command` in `tests/test_cli_seed.py` were run against the pre-edit tree: the two `tasks`-command tests failed with exit code 2 (no such command), and the third failed on the still-present `skeptic tasks list` string in `find_task`'s error. `tests/test_spec.py`'s `test_missing_file_is_infra_error_with_next_command` asserted `match="skeptic tasks list"` against `load_task`'s file-not-found message (a second dangling reference the brief's file list didn't name but the grep it asked for turned up); updated to `match="skeptic tasks"` alongside the string it pins. All three new/changed assertions pass after the edit: `python -m pytest -q tests/test_cli_tasks.py tests/test_cli_seed.py tests/test_spec.py`: 39 passed.

**Grep before and after.** Before: `grep -rn "tasks list" --include="*.py" .` hit four lines (`skeptic/spec.py:255`, `:284`; `tests/test_spec.py:50`; `tests/test_cli_seed.py:36`). After: one hit, `tests/test_cli_seed.py`'s own `assert "skeptic tasks list" not in result.output`, which names the retired string on purpose to pin its absence. `docs/skeptic-engineering-plan.md` still carries the old name in two places, both left untouched on review (owner ruling: the master plan is a v3-frozen record, amendments route through DECISIONS rows by this project's own convention, not through silent edits to the plan body): line 211's discoverability bullet (`` `skeptic tasks list`, `skeptic runs list` ``) and line 223's module-layout comment inside the `cli.py` tree entry (`` # typer: seed build verify run eval report demo doctor clean; tasks/runs list ``); both also name `runs list`, a command that doesn't exist yet either, so neither line documents shipped behavior.

**Review round fixes.** Three findings from review, one important and two minor. Important: `skeptic tasks`'s command body had no `try/except SkepticInfraError` wrapper (row 165 above documents the fix and the reasoning). Minor: `tests/test_spec.py`'s `match="skeptic tasks"` was a substring match that would also have matched the retired `"skeptic tasks list"` wording; tightened to `` match=r"`skeptic tasks` to see available task ids" `` (the exact text following the fix, anchored past the point where the two strings diverge) plus a standalone `assert "tasks list" not in str(excinfo.value)`, mirroring `test_cli_seed.py`'s own dual-assertion rigor. Minor: `tests/test_cli_tasks.py`'s `assert "acceptance" in result.output` passed under both the acceptance and no-acceptance-suite branches, since `"no acceptance suite"` also contains the substring `"acceptance"`; tightened to `assert "· acceptance" in result.output` (the exact marker text, which only the positive branch renders) plus `assert "no acceptance suite" not in result.output` against the real corpus, and a new `test_tasks_marks_a_task_with_no_acceptance_suite` exercises the negative branch directly against a tmp-dir task built from `valid-task.yaml` (which carries no `acceptance_suite` key). The important fix's test, `test_tasks_on_a_corrupt_yaml_reports_infra_error_not_a_traceback`, was run against the pre-fix command first and failed with exit code 1 (the CliRunner-visible face of the uncaught exception), the predicted red.

Full fast suite (`python -m pytest -q -m "not docker"`): 632 passed, 81 deselected (+5 over the pre-task baseline of 627: three from the first pass, two more from the review round's new corpus-yaml and no-acceptance-suite tests). `ruff check .`: clean.
